## Decision 092 — A New Safeguard-Removal Signature Appears in Western Pacific Contingency Planning

Selection: A.

Status: Locked.

Decision: The commander’s closing threat beat is an evidence-centered discovery rather than a commander POV or covert handoff scene. After Cascadia is defeated and the commander escapes with an incomplete portable capability, a protected review identifies an anomaly in Western Pacific maritime and logistics contingency planning.

The source records and inputs are authentic. One inconvenient scenario has been demoted because a safeguard that should preserve, elevate, or compel review of it was removed, reordered, or functionally weakened. The pattern is technically consistent with the safeguard-removal doctrine proved through the scientist’s full debrief.

Proof ceiling: The signature does not identify the operator, commander, organization, approval chain, country, location, or intent. It does not prove that the commander personally altered or ordered alteration of the product. It does not prove operational deployment, a completed attack, or actual manipulation of blockade decisions.

Permitted inference: The incomplete pattern supports the bounded assessment that someone possessing derivative knowledge from at least part of the stolen package has begun adapting the doctrine to the developing Western Pacific blockade environment. The anomaly does not replace the missing concordance ledger or establish the complete relationship map.

Public-record rule: The anomaly remains protected investigative and assessment material and is not added to the bounded Book 2 public summary. It cannot be used to broaden the public finding or support attribution beyond admissible or formally established evidence.

Technology rule: No system is sentient or self-directing. The anomaly results from human decisions about validation, ranking, escalation, review, and presentation safeguards.

Narrative reason: This choice demonstrates that the escaped commander retained a useful but incomplete capability, connects Book 2 to the next geopolitical threat, preserves the commander’s identity mystery, and keeps the final antagonist beat grounded in provenance and proof limits rather than a villain monologue.

Canon consequences: The commander remains at large; the responsible operator and institutional relationship remain unresolved; the Western Pacific blockade environment becomes the next active threat horizon; and the final human scene may remain focused on Julie, Elias, the scientist, and the independent institute. The exact planning body, scenario, discovery mechanism, timing, and discovering character remain open.

## Decision 093 — Book 2 Ends in Julie POV as Elias Formally Joins the Institute

Selection: A.

Status: Locked.

Decision: The protected Western Pacific safeguard-removal anomaly is the final continuing-threat beat, but it is not the novel’s last scene. Book 2 ends with a human aftermath scene in Julie O’Donnell’s close-third POV at the scientist-led independent institute’s provisional workspace.

Scene sequence: Julie attends through a lawful and bounded connection to the closed Cascadia work and the next protected inquiry. She offers Elias a narrowly defined role with the current purpose, scope, access limits, and known risks disclosed. Elias declines because she continued using his compartmented work after the conditions of purpose and risk materially changed without renewing informed consent.

Rupture rule: Elias acknowledges that Julie’s secrecy was operationally defensible. His refusal rests on the loss of meaningful consent, not on a claim that she acted irrationally, on romantic grievance, on disbelief in her mission, or on a false misunderstanding that could be resolved through one explanation.

Institute action: The scientist presents the transparent employment agreement established by Decisions 089 and 091. Elias reviews and signs as Founding Director of Systems Audit. The scientist countersigns as founder and ultimate technical authority.

Employment protections: Elias receives renewable consent, disclosure when material purpose or risk changes, documented scope and source limits, and an enforceable stop-work right. His role grants no automatic government access, evidence custody, classification authority, investigative power, intelligence-assessment ownership, or public-attribution authority.

Julie’s ending state: Julie recognizes that she helped make the institute possible but does not own it, control it, employ Elias, or direct its future work. She and Elias neither reconcile nor depart in open hostility. Future collaboration remains possible only through a new lawful mandate, transparent boundaries, and renewed consent.

Scientist and daughter rule: The scientist retains leadership over the institute and her work. Her daughter may support the moment personally but is not converted into institute staff, governance, counsel, investigative support, spokesperson, or compulsory caregiver.

Final image: The final narrative perspective remains with Julie as the scientist and Elias begin defining their first independent audit under terms Julie does not control and Julie leaves them to work.

Narrative reason: This ending keeps the series protagonist in the final POV, delivers the personal consequence of Julie’s compartmentation, makes Elias’s choice formal and autonomous, and closes the book on a functioning alternative built from the story’s failures rather than on the escaped commander.

Canon consequences: No commander POV, covert handoff, or later antagonist coda follows. Elias formally joins the institute before the novel ends. The Julie–Elias rupture remains unresolved but potentially recoverable. The institute remains independent from Julie and both governments. The exact location, timing, staging, dialogue, first audit assignment, and Julie’s next mandate remain open.

## Decision 094 — Julie Accepts a New Binational Case-Specific Independent Mandate

Selection: A.

Status: Locked.

Decision: Following discovery of the protected Western Pacific safeguard-removal signature, designated Canadian and United States authorities jointly issue Julie a new written, case-specific independent mandate. She accepts it before leaving the institute’s provisional workspace.

Instrument rule: The mandate is newly constituted and is not an extension of the compromised commission, a permanent government position, a general intelligence credential, or command authority over Canadian or United States investigators.

Defined scope: Julie may reconstruct chronology and provenance, test source integrity, compare authenticated inputs with decision products, identify removed or weakened safeguards, define proof ceilings, and frame bounded evidentiary questions concerning the affected Western Pacific contingency-planning chain.

Custody rule: Each country retains lawful custody and control of its own evidence, intelligence, witness records, and systems. Julie’s appointment does not merge sovereign evidence holdings, transfer original custody, bypass domestic legal process, or permit unrestricted cross-border access.

Access rule: Every protected source requires specific authorization for the defined inquiry. Julie receives no automatic access to unrelated intelligence, criminal-investigation files, operational systems, or the missing concordance ledger.

Authority limits: Julie cannot compel testimony, subpoena records, search, arrest, seize, classify, declassify, command operations, determine prosecution, certify scientific findings, or make public attribution. She remains an independent evidence and provenance specialist.

Integrity clause: Material changes in scope, purpose, access, monitoring, disclosure risk, or expected use of Julie’s work must be documented to her. She may renew, narrow, suspend, or decline the mandate rather than continue under silently changed conditions.

Institute separation: The scientist-led nonprofit and Elias are not part of Julie’s mandate, are not automatically retained, and receive no derivative protected access. Future cooperation requires separate lawful custody terms, transparent scope, risk disclosure, and consent. Elias declines to participate through Julie’s structure and remains the institute’s Founding Director of Systems Audit.

Proof ceiling: The mandate responds to a bounded technical signature and continuing risk. It does not identify the commander, responsible operator, organization, state relationship, operational deployment, or completed attack.

Narrative reason: The decision gives Julie a clear lawful Book 3 direction, preserves her independent status and binational evidence discipline, contrasts her institutional path with Elias’s nonprofit role, and prevents the original compromised commission from becoming her continuing authority.

Canon consequences: Julie leaves Book 2 with a concrete protected assignment and earned institutional credibility while remaining publicly unnamed. Canada and the United States co-sponsor the inquiry but retain separate sovereign authority and custody. The sponsoring offices, named leads, compensation, duration, initial records, and first investigative step remain open.

## Decision 095 — National-Security Coordinators Sponsor Julie’s Mandate While Investigative Leads Control Evidence

Selection: A.

Status: Locked.

Decision: Julie’s new bilateral mandate is jointly issued and governed at the policy-coordination level by the Privy Council Office National Security Council Secretariat and a designated United States National Security Council staff counterpart.

This is a new written mandate rather than an extension of the compromised joint commission. The commission retains no operational, sponsorship, evidence-custody, or tasking authority over Julie’s next inquiry.

Sponsor authority: The two national-security coordinators define the approved case-specific scope, coordinate cross-border access requests, resolve policy-level conflicts, protect classified sources and methods, receive bounded findings, and maintain the authoritative mandate record.

Consent and scope rule: Any material change to purpose, risk, access, reporting audience, or intended use must be disclosed to Julie. Material expansion requires joint written sponsor approval and Julie’s renewed acceptance. Fragmented tasking cannot be used to conceal a broader purpose.

Evidence-authority rule: The RCMP co-lead retains Canadian evidence, witness, custody, and disclosure authority. The designated United States investigative lead retains American evidence, witness, custody, and disclosure authority. Each lead applies its own law and protection requirements.

The policy sponsors cannot transfer or merge national evidence, compel testimony, direct searches or arrests, override lawful custody or witness-protection limits, grant Julie general intelligence access, or turn coordinated policy judgment into evidentiary proof.

When an access request is refused, narrowed, delayed, or answered through an authenticated extract or derived summary, the restriction and resulting proof ceiling must be recorded. Policy sponsors may coordinate lawful alternatives but cannot erase the limitation.

Repository rule: Canadian and United States source repositories remain separate. Julie may perform authorized comparison without creating a merged binational truth file or assuming custody of original evidence.

Reporting rule: Julie separates authenticated evidence, provenance, derived information, institutional positions, technical inference, unresolved disagreement, and unknowns. Sponsors may protect classified details but may not rewrite her findings or add unsupported attribution.

Institutional-separation rule: The scientist-led nonprofit and Elias remain outside the mandate. Neither receives automatic access or tasking, and neither is obligated to work for Julie or either government.

Narrative reason: The structure gives Julie realistic cross-border reach while preserving evidence custody, national legal authority, proof discipline, and her independence. It also creates continuing tension between policy officials seeking rapid answers and investigators responsible for lawful evidence protection.

Canon consequences: Julie commands no government personnel and holds no arrest, compulsion, classification, or public-accusation authority. The commission does not sponsor the next case. The precise sponsor representatives, U.S. investigative lead, reporting cadence, suspension mechanism, and first authorized objective remain unresolved.

## Decision 096 — Julie’s First Authorized Objective Is to Trace the Recurring Approval Function into the Western Pacific Planning Chain

Selection: A.

Status: Locked.

Decision: Julie’s first authorized objective under the new binational mandate is to determine whether the recurring internal approval function identified through the scientist’s full debrief has a demonstrable relationship to the Western Pacific safeguard-removal signature.

Required alternative hypotheses: The inquiry must distinguish among the same approval function acting again; a successor or related compartment inheriting its methods; an unconnected actor copying the doctrine without sharing the original command structure; and a coincidental, incomplete, or deliberately planted similarity.

Initial-scope rule: Julie begins with provenance, chronology, workflow, and approval-path reconstruction. Her task is not initially a commander manhunt, national-intent assessment, state attribution, or full operational-impact review.

Permitted source categories include authenticated versions, revision histories, approval-routing records, safeguard definitions, access records, release and distribution records, lawfully available witness accounts, and clearly labeled derived summaries. Canadian and United States investigative leads retain their separate evidence and witness authority.

Proof rule: Technical resemblance alone does not establish common control. A linkage requires authenticated procedural, chronological, access, revision, or provenance relationships that remain credible after alternative explanations and source-integrity challenges are tested.

First deliverable: Julie produces a bounded provenance-and-chronology assessment identifying established relationships, disproved or unsupported links, viable competing explanations, additional records required, access restrictions affecting the proof ceiling, and whether the evidence supports requesting a broader operational or attribution review.

Role-versus-person rule: The assessment may identify a recurring function, workflow, or institutional interface without identifying its holder. A role match is not a person match and does not establish that the escaped commander controlled the Western Pacific anomaly.

Institute-separation rule: Any technical assistance from the scientist-led nonprofit requires a separate voluntary and documented agreement consistent with institute governance and consent protections. Elias is not compelled to participate, and Julie may not use her government mandate to bypass his refusal.

Narrative reason: This objective connects Cascadia’s strongest evidence to the next threat while preserving Julie’s evidence-specialist role, national custody boundaries, attribution discipline, and the commander’s unresolved identity. It creates investigative pressure through missing records, disputed access, altered approval histories, institutional resistance, and potential false linkage.

Canon consequences: The recurring approval function is the first investigative lead but not a proven continuing actor. The commander’s identity, hierarchy, state relationship, location, and control of the Western Pacific anomaly remain unresolved. The exact opening records, witnesses, markers, access conflict, and escalation threshold remain open.

## Decision 097 — Julie May Escalate Only After Proving Both an Authenticated Lineage Link and Live or Imminent Operational Use

Selection: A.

Status: Locked.

Decision: Expansion of Julie’s initial provenance-and-chronology mandate into an urgent protected operational review requires two independently authenticated elements: a lineage relationship to the recurring approval function or derivative safeguard-removal workflow, and evidence that the affected planning product is live or imminently consequential.

Lineage requirement: Julie must establish an authenticated procedural, provenance, access, revision, approval-path, or derivative relationship. Technical resemblance, similar wording, matching policy preferences, or unsupported analytic judgment is insufficient.

Operational-use requirement: Julie must establish that the altered product has entered, is scheduled to enter, or has materially influenced a live decision channel, including a consequential briefing, recommendation, allocation, deployment, readiness, logistics, or comparable process.

Conjunctive rule: Both elements are mandatory for escalation under Julie’s mandate. Lineage alone permits continued investigation and lawful record-preservation requests. Operational relevance alone may justify a separate safety review by competent authorities but does not prove a relationship to the recurring approval function.

Escalation request: Julie must document the sources, authentication status, plausible alternatives, time sensitivity, proof ceilings, requested access, and proposed protective measures. The two national-security sponsors determine whether to expand the written mandate.

Permitted expanded measures may include accelerated binational access coordination, lawful preservation action by competent authorities, protected warnings, a bounded operational-impact assessment, expedited witness or record protection, and specific additional access to the affected decision chain.

Authority limit: Julie cannot herself issue preservation orders, compel records or testimony, warn operational units in an official capacity, direct deployments, or command investigators. Each action must be authorized and executed by the legally competent national authority.

Proof ceiling: Satisfying the two-part gate does not identify the escaped commander, prove personal control, establish state or institutional responsibility, create criminal liability, or support public attribution. Those remain separate determinations.

Emergency exception: A competent authority may issue a safety warning based on its own information before Julie satisfies both elements. Such action does not retroactively establish lineage or permit officials to characterize Julie’s work as having proved it.

Consent rule: Any material mandate expansion must be written, disclosed, and accepted by Julie. The institute and Elias remain outside the inquiry unless they separately consent under their own governance safeguards.

Narrative reason: The threshold preserves Julie’s evidence discipline while creating parallel races to prove origin and determine whether the manipulation is about to affect real decisions.

Canon consequences: Lineage, operational danger, criminal proof, intelligence attribution, and public attribution remain distinct thresholds. The evidence satisfying each element, the sequence in which they are met, the escalation time window, the responding authorities, and the first protective action remain unresolved.

## Decision 098 — A Retired Approval-Routing Identifier Reappears in the Western Pacific Revision Record

Selection: A.

Status: Locked.

Decision: Julie’s first authenticated lineage link is a retired approval-routing identifier associated with the legitimate pilot’s recurring approval pathway. The identifier appears in authenticated revision metadata for the anomalous Western Pacific planning product.

Authentication rule: The legitimate pilot record must establish the identifier’s former role and formal retirement. The Western Pacific occurrence must be verified within the authoritative native revision chain using available integrity controls, time records, custody history, and audit or signing evidence. A screenshot, transcription, copied label, or later summary is insufficient.

Permitted conclusion: The identifier establishes a technical and procedural relationship between the Western Pacific revision and the retired route. It does not determine whether the original route was restored, whether credentials or configuration were cloned, whether a successor compartment inherited the identifier, or whether an actor deliberately planted it.

Planting rule: A deliberately planted identifier may establish that an actor had sufficient access or knowledge to place the marker in the authenticated record. It does not prove that the original route processed the revision or that the planting actor performed, directed, or approved the safeguard change.

Proof ceiling: The identifier does not identify its user, a human approver, the escaped commander, an organization, a government, a location, or a motive. It does not prove common command, state responsibility, criminal liability, or public attribution.

Escalation consequence: This decision satisfies only the authenticated-lineage component of Decision 097. It does not establish live or imminent operational use. Julie may seek related preservation and access through lawful authorities, but urgent operational expansion remains unavailable until the second component is independently authenticated.

Authorized follow-up records may include retirement approvals, route and role mappings, identity bindings, configuration inventories, administrative histories, restoration or change requests, successor documentation, audit gaps, protected backups, and access logs.

Conflict rule: Inconsistencies between the retirement record and the Western Pacific metadata remain recorded conflicts. Missing mappings, inaccessible records, ambiguous identifier reuse, and denied requests become explicit proof limits rather than grounds for inference.

Technology rule: The route and identifier are non-sentient mechanisms. Any restoration, cloning, inheritance, reuse, or planting is the result of human action.

Narrative reason: This creates a concrete documentary bridge between the legitimate pilot, the Cascadia doctrine, and the Western Pacific anomaly without collapsing a routing marker into personal, organizational, or state attribution.

Canon consequences: The lineage half of the escalation threshold is fulfilled; the operational-use half is not. The exact identifier, repository, custodian, retirement authority, discovery route, and ultimate explanation remain open.

## Decision 099 — An Authenticated Briefing Manifest Establishes Imminent Operational Use

Selection: A.

Status: Locked.

Decision: Julie obtains an authenticated release and distribution manifest proving that the altered Western Pacific planning product has been approved for inclusion in a restricted senior-level contingency briefing scheduled within hours.

Authentication requirement: The manifest must be part of the official briefing-production record and must connect the scheduled packet to the specific altered product through sufficient packet, version, revision, custody, or integrity identifiers. An informal schedule, draft circulation list, analyst expectation, or unauthenticated screenshot is insufficient.

Operational-use finding: Placement of the altered product in the scheduled senior decision packet establishes imminent entry into a live human decision channel. Combined with the authenticated lineage link established in Decision 098, it satisfies both mandatory elements of the Decision 097 escalation threshold.

Proof ceiling: The manifest does not prove that the packet was opened, fully distributed, presented, trusted, accepted, relied upon, or used to make an operational or policy decision. It does not prove that recipients, briefing personnel, packet staff, or senior decision-makers knew about or participated in the manipulation.

Human-authority rule: The briefing and any resulting policy, military, intelligence, diplomatic, or logistics decision remain human governed. The altered product may influence judgment but cannot autonomously issue or compel an order.

Authorized consequence: Julie may submit an urgent protected request to expand her work into operational-impact review and may identify the affected product, integrity risk, proof ceiling, and records requiring preservation. The lawful packet custodian and decision authority—not Julie—determine the immediate intervention.

Authority limit: Julie cannot cancel the briefing, remove or replace packet material, compel recipients, direct government operations, seize evidence, or issue public attribution.

Preservation rule: The original packet, manifest, distribution state, altered product, approval history, and all corrective actions must be preserved. Preventing exposure cannot be allowed to erase evidence of how the product entered the decision channel.

Attribution rule: Packet placement proves imminent exposure only. It does not identify the person who inserted or approved the product, establish intent, identify the escaped commander, or prove organizational or state responsibility.

Narrative reason: This choice creates a precise ticking clock while preserving the distinctions among scheduled exposure, completed distribution, actual briefing, decision influence, and executed action. Julie can lawfully escalate before irreversible consequences without becoming the operational decision-maker.

Canon consequences: Decisions 098 and 099 together meet Decision 097’s escalation gate. The specific briefing body, jurisdiction, packet custodian, recipients, authentication method, exact schedule, and first protective action remain unresolved.

## Decision 100 — The Altered Product Is Placed Under an Evidence-Preserving Hold and a Protected Integrity Notice Is Issued

Selection: A.

Status: Locked.

Decision: Once the retired approval-routing identifier and authenticated briefing manifest satisfy both elements of Decision 097, the lawful packet custodian places a temporary evidence-preserving hold on the altered planning product and issues a protected integrity notice to the lawful senior decision authority.

Scope of hold: The hold applies only to the affected product unless additional evidence independently justifies broader action. It suspends that product from distribution, presentation, or reliance but does not automatically cancel the entire briefing or freeze unrelated planning.

Preservation rule: Before correction, removal, substitution, or annotation, the custodian preserves the original packet state, authenticated manifest, distribution records, recipient records, altered product, prior versions, revision and approval history, authorized access logs, integrity markers, relevant timestamps, and the formal hold action.

No silent-substitution rule: The altered item may not be deleted or overwritten. Any later replacement, prior valid version, qualification, or uncertainty notice must be authenticated, clearly identified, separately versioned, and recorded in the packet history.

Integrity-notice rule: The notice separates authenticated facts, technical findings, bounded inference, unresolved alternatives, proof ceilings, and attribution not established. It explains why the product cannot presently be relied upon without accusing packet staff, briefing officials, the escaped commander, an organization, or a state.

Exposure proof ceiling: Placement in the scheduled packet proves imminent entry into a live decision channel. It does not prove that the product was opened, presented, accepted, trusted, relied upon, or used to make a decision.

Decision-authority rule: The lawful senior authority determines whether the wider briefing proceeds and approves any independently authenticated substitute, preserved prior valid version, or neutral uncertainty statement. Julie does not choose strategy, policy, military posture, logistics, or operational recommendations.

Julie’s role: Julie authenticates the integrity defect, explains the provenance failure and proof limits, identifies additional records needed, and may assess whether a proposed substitute has an independent documented lineage. She does not exercise packet custody or command authority.

Nonpunitive rule: A hold protects decisions and evidence; it does not establish misconduct, guilt, criminal liability, hostile control, or knowledge by anyone in the distribution chain.

No-bait rule: The compromised product cannot remain in the senior packet as covert investigative bait. Any counterintelligence or criminal use requires separate lawful authority and cannot expose decision-makers to material already judged unreliable.

Narrative reason: The intervention prevents immediate harm while preserving the chain needed to investigate the approval network. It demonstrates institutional learning, keeps legitimate planning moving, and confines Julie to her evidence-specialist role.

Canon consequences: The original packet and altered item survive intact as evidence; the senior authority receives a bounded integrity warning; the wider briefing may continue through independently authenticated material; and packet placement cannot be inflated into proof of reliance or attribution. The custodian, senior authority, substitute material, resistance mechanism, and exact briefing clock remain unresolved.

## Decision 101 — The Held Product Is Replaced by an Independent Source-Record Reconstruction with an Explicit Uncertainty Annex

Selection: A.

Status: Locked.

Decision: The product held under Decision 100 is not corrected in place or used as the analytical foundation for its own replacement. A separately authorized and appropriately cleared team reconstructs the relevant Western Pacific scenario comparison directly from authenticated source records.

Independence rule: No person who drafted, ranked, weighted, approved, routed, or released the held product may direct the reconstruction or control its findings. Bounded subject-matter assistance is permitted only when the contribution and any conflict are recorded.

Clean-room rule: Until its work is sealed, the reconstruction team cannot view or reuse the held product’s rankings, weights, framing, hidden assumptions, intermediate calculations, confidence labels, safeguard decisions, or strategic conclusion. It receives a neutral task statement, authenticated source inventory, lawful access instructions, and the senior decision authority’s planning questions.

Evidence-preservation rule: The held product, manifest, revision history, approval trail, distribution records, and related artifacts remain sequestered and unchanged. The reconstruction does not annotate, overwrite, normalize, or otherwise alter the preserved evidence.

Source rule: Every used source requires documented provenance, access authority, version identity, and handling history. Extracts and derived summaries must be labeled. Missing, delayed, disputed, inaccessible, stale, or differently classified sources remain explicit limitations.

No-assumption rule: The reconstruction cannot import assumptions from the held product to fill gaps. When authenticated sources do not support a reproducible comparison, the team must state that no reliable ranking can be produced.

Uncertainty-annex rule: The replacement must identify unavailable sources, source cutoffs, unresolved assumptions, material disagreements, sensitivity to alternative interpretations, nonreproducible claims, and conclusions below the lawful confidence threshold.

Julie’s role: Julie verifies provenance, source boundaries, team independence, reproducibility, contamination controls, and proof ceilings. She may reject unsupported certainty or a failure to distinguish fact, inference, and policy judgment. She does not choose weights, dictate the strategic conclusion, select military or diplomatic action, or certify more than the evidence supports.

Decision-authority rule: The lawful senior decision authority decides whether the reconstruction, a narrowed portion, or only the uncertainty annex enters the briefing. Any inclusion receives a new manifest entry and cannot be described as a restored or corrected version of the held product.

Institute-separation rule: Elias and the scientist-led nonprofit are not compelled to participate. Later involvement requires a separate written engagement, lawful access, conflict review, and voluntary acceptance under institute safeguards.

Fallback rule: If the reconstruction cannot be completed defensibly before the briefing, officials use a neutral integrity and uncertainty notice rather than an improvised ranking or disguised reuse of the held product.

Narrative reason: The clean-room reconstruction creates a deadline-driven analytical problem while proving that source integrity requires rebuilding from authenticated evidence rather than cosmetically repairing a suspect conclusion.

Canon consequences: The replacement may be narrower, less polished, and more uncertain than the held product. Its substantive conclusion, team, source set, completion timing, format, and analytical disagreements remain unresolved. The reconstruction proves no sabotage, commander involvement, organizational control, criminal liability, or state attribution.

## Decision 102 — The Independent Reconstruction Restores a Suppressed High-Consequence Logistics Cascade to Mandatory Senior Review

Selection: A.

Status: Locked.

Decision: The independent source-record reconstruction establishes that the held Western Pacific planning product improperly demoted a lower-probability but high-consequence logistics cascade involving commercial-port throughput, fuel distribution, repair capacity, civilian supply movement, and military sustainment.

Clean-room rule: The reconstruction is produced from authenticated source records without access to or reuse of the held product’s ranking, weights, framing, assumptions, calculations, confidence labels, safeguard decisions, or strategic conclusion.

Safeguard result: Restoring legitimate tail-risk retention, dependency propagation, source-conflict visibility, and consequence-based senior-review requirements causes the cascade to cross the threshold for mandatory senior consideration.

Mandatory-review rule: The cascade does not thereby become certain, most likely, or controlling. Mandatory review requires visibility and explicit consideration; it does not compel a policy, military, commercial, or diplomatic response.

Uncertainty rule: The annex records unavailable or stale sources, unresolved assumptions, conflicting records, unsupported probability precision, and dependencies that could not be fully authenticated within the briefing deadline. Missing values are not silently estimated or inherited from the held product.

Technical finding: The held product’s defect can be established without alleging fabricated source facts. Authentic inputs became misleading through omission, demotion, weighting, sequencing, broken relationship propagation, or removal of review safeguards.

Proof ceiling: The reconstruction does not identify the person who altered the product, establish motive or intent, prove that the escaped commander controlled the alteration, or attribute the act to an organization, government, or state. It does not prove that a blockade, attack, logistics failure, or cascading disruption will occur.

Julie’s authority: Julie verifies provenance, clean-room separation, reproducibility boundaries, and proof limits. She does not set the analytical ranking, choose strategy, direct military posture, instruct commercial operators, or make the senior policy decision.

Senior-authority rule: The lawful decision authority determines whether and how to respond after receiving the reconstruction and uncertainty annex. The analysis supports review, not prediction or accusation.

Narrative reason: The decision gives safeguard removal a concrete strategic consequence while maintaining uncertainty, lawful human control, and the series’ evidence-first discipline. It creates an actionable Western Pacific pressure without resolving the commander or wider network.

Canon consequences: The cascade must be explicitly reviewed because its authenticated dependencies and possible consequences exceed the legitimate omission threshold. Its exact probability, timeline, geography, initiating event, facilities, and response remain unresolved. The reconstruction remains protected and is not itself a public warning or attribution product.

## Decision 103 — The Senior Authority Authorizes Time-Limited No-Regret Resilience and Verification Measures

Selection: A.

Status: Locked.

Decision: After receiving the independent source-record reconstruction and its uncertainty annex, the lawful senior decision authority authorizes a bounded package of time-limited no-regret resilience and verification measures addressing the restored high-consequence logistics cascade.

Proof rule: The authorization is precautionary. It does not establish that the cascade is the most likely outcome, will occur, is imminent, was deliberately engineered, or is attributable to the escaped commander, a named organization, or a state.

Authorized scope: Responsible authorities may verify alternate commercial-port and cargo-routing capacity; confirm fuel inventories, distribution paths, dependencies, and lawful priority rules; test repair-facility access, replacement components, transport capacity, and qualified personnel; review continuity plans for civilian essentials and military sustainment; authenticate the source records and assumptions used by participating organizations; and preserve all material records.

Authority rule: Each government, regulator, military organization, and commercial operator retains its lawful powers, custody, obligations, and decision rights. The directive coordinates objectives and reporting but does not place Julie in operational command or merge national, military, regulatory, or commercial authority.

Duration and review rule: Measures must be proportionate, reversible where practical, documented, independently reviewable, and subject to a defined reassessment point. Continuation, expansion, or conversion into permanent policy requires a separately recorded lawful basis.

Prohibited expansion: The directive does not authorize a public warning, public accusation, blockade declaration, offensive action, covert provocation, major force repositioning, compulsory commercial seizure, or market intervention solely on the basis of the reconstruction.

Julie’s role: Julie may audit provenance, assumptions, evidence boundaries, implementation records, and reported outcomes. She cannot choose military posture, commercial priorities, resource allocation, or policy outcomes and cannot compel implementation by an authority or private operator.

Evidence-preservation rule: The held product, reconstruction, uncertainty annex, integrity notice, authorization, implementation records, and verification results remain separately identifiable. Protective action cannot overwrite the evidence or retroactively validate the compromised product.

Interpretation rule: Results must separate tested capacity from asserted capacity, current fact from contingency estimate, ordinary fragility from unexplained anomaly, and resilience improvement from proof of hostile interference.

Institute rule: The scientist-led nonprofit and Elias remain outside the directive absent a separately disclosed and accepted engagement governed by institute independence and individual consent.

Narrative reason: This choice creates concrete protective action and expands the evidence surface into ports, fuel networks, repair capacity, civilian continuity systems, and military sustainment without treating an uncertain scenario as prophecy or triggering premature geopolitical escalation.

Canon consequences: The response remains protected and below the threshold for public attribution or major military action. Exact workstream leaders, duration, geographic scope, private-sector participants, review interval, first verified weakness, and first sign of obstruction remain unresolved.

## Decision 104 — Supposedly Independent Backup Routes Converge on One Commercial Fuel-and-Repair Node

Selection: A.

Status: Locked.

Decision: The first weakness established by the time-limited resilience review is phantom redundancy. Continuity plans maintained by separate port, fuel, transport, repair, and sustainment authorities claim independent alternatives that ultimately rely on the same privately operated coastal node.

Apparent-independence rule: Immediate carriers, contracts, route labels, administrative owners, and responsible organizations differ. Those surface differences caused the plans to be treated as independent when reviewed separately.

Dependency finding: Julie’s authenticated cross-system reconstruction shows convergence on one or more indispensable services at the shared node, including fuel transfer, cargo transshipment, specialized repair access, component staging, qualified technical personnel, or onward transportation. The final functional mix remains open.

Scale rule: The node is consequential but is not a single switch controlling the region. Other facilities and routes exist. The defect is that several nominal backups share a critical failure point, causing planners to understate correlated disruption.

Evidence basis: The finding must rest on authenticated contracts, route and transfer records, access agreements, capacity assumptions, repair authorizations, component records, staffing requirements, and onward-transport dependencies. Stale assumptions and unresolved mismatches remain explicitly labeled.

Proof ceiling: The node is lawful and may be operating normally. Shared dependency does not prove sabotage, hostile ownership, compromised personnel, covert control, intentional concealment, or deliberate creation of the vulnerability. Ordinary market concentration, contracting convenience, institutional fragmentation, and planning drift remain viable explanations.

Attribution rule: The finding does not identify the escaped commander, recurring approval function, government, organization, operator, or individual as responsible. It does not prove attempted or intended exploitation.

Protective-authority rule: Lawful operational authorities may verify true alternate capacity, access, throughput, staffing, component availability, and restoration time and may implement reversible measures under Decision 103. Julie audits supporting evidence and provenance but does not direct logistics or commercial operations.

Intrusion limit: The dependency alone does not authorize seizure, public accusation, designation of the operator as compromised, unrelated compulsory disclosure, or covert surveillance. Any intrusive measure requires separate lawful grounds.

Custody rule: Original commercial, regulatory, military, and government records remain with lawful custodians. Julie may compare authenticated records or controlled extracts without creating an ungoverned merged repository.

Narrative reason: The finding makes the logistics cascade concrete, rewards Julie’s evidence method, and opens contracts, access agreements, repair capacity, staffing, and source assumptions as investigative paths while preserving ordinary and hostile explanations.

Canon consequences: The first verified resilience weakness is a commercial fuel-and-repair node shared by supposedly independent backup plans. It is a structural vulnerability, not proof of hostile action. Exact geography, operator, ownership, functions, discovery sequence, corrective alternative, and first evidence of exploitation remain unresolved.

## Decision 105 — Routine-Looking Contract Changes Create a Near-Term Concurrency Window at the Shared Node

Selection: A.

Status: Locked.

Decision: Julie’s first credible concern that the shared commercial fuel-and-repair node may be positioned for exploitation arises from an authenticated cross-record chronology of individually lawful commercial changes that converge during one narrow future window.

Change set: The chronology includes a scheduled fuel-transfer capacity reduction, specialist repair-team reassignment, replacement-component staging relocation, temporary cargo-handling restriction, and alternate-carrier availability change. Exact entities, mechanisms, quantities, dates, and duration remain open.

Individual-validity rule: Each change is documented, facially lawful, approved through an ordinary process, and supported by a plausible independent reason. No individual approval establishes corruption, hostile control, or deception.

Fragmentation rule: The changes were submitted at different times through separate contracting, staffing, maintenance, logistics, and transportation channels. Each approving body saw its own transaction rather than the complete dependency map established in Decision 104.

Combined-effect finding: Julie authenticates that the schedules overlap. If all changes proceed, several supposedly independent capabilities lose meaningful capacity simultaneously and converge on a substantially degraded shared node.

Proof ceiling: The overlap proves a concurrency window and foreseeable combined vulnerability. It does not prove common authorship, coordination, malicious intent, knowledge of the combined effect, node compromise, commander involvement, organizational control, or state direction.

Alternative explanations remain live, including coincidence, common market conditions, seasonal demand, maintenance needs, labor limits, insurance requirements, ordinary cost reductions, and institutional failure to reconcile separate plans.

Permitted response: The finding supports urgent preservation of original requests, approvals, amendments, communications, capacity records, stated business reasons, and implementation schedules. It also supports a cross-owner deconfliction and reversible protective review under the no-regret mandate.

Authority boundary: Julie may identify the combined evidence and resulting proof ceiling. She cannot cancel a contract, compel a private operator, order operational changes, accuse any participant, or label the pattern hostile. Any pause, rescheduling, substitution, or emergency arrangement requires separate lawful authority and a recorded decision-maker.

Inference-labeling rule: Verified dates, submissions, approvals, and capacity effects must remain distinct from the hypothesis that someone may be positioning the node for exploitation. The hypothesis is a protected investigative lead, not an attribution finding.

Technology rule: No sentient system coordinates these events. Any coordination, concealment, exploitation, or administrative failure is attributable to human action, human approval, or fragmented human institutions.

Narrative reason: The concurrence creates a precise ticking clock before disruption begins, rewards Julie’s cross-system provenance method, and gives authorities a lawful reason to intervene without pretending that suspicion equals proof.

Canon consequences: The overlap and combined degradation are authenticated. Coordination, common purpose, knowledge, intent, criminality, commander involvement, and state attribution remain unresolved. The exact window and first lawful protective action remain undecided.

## Decision 106 — Lawful Authorities Impose a Coordinated Time-Limited Standstill on Only the Overlapping Degradation Changes

Selection: A.

Status: Locked.

Decision: The Canadian and United States national-security sponsors coordinate a protected deconfliction notice identifying the authenticated changes that collectively create the near-term concurrency window. The notice has coordination and warning force only; it does not create pooled binational regulatory, contractual, or commercial authority.

Separate-authority rule: Each competent authority acts through its own existing law, contract, regulation, or operational responsibility and records its own lawful basis for temporarily delaying the change under its control.

Scope rule: The pause applies only to the overlapping capacity-reducing changes—the fuel-transfer reduction, specialist repair-team reassignment, component-staging relocation, temporary cargo-handling restriction, alternate-carrier availability change, and any later-authenticated equivalent that reproduces the same overlap.

Continuity rule: Ordinary node operations continue. The standstill does not cancel the underlying agreements, invalidate prior approvals, suspend unrelated work, or transfer commercial control to Julie, the policy sponsors, or the investigative leads.

Nonpunitive rule: The intervention is a protective response to a structural risk. It does not accuse or sanction the node operator, commercial parties, approving officials, or employees.

Evidence-preservation rule: Before a change is delayed, the responsible custodian preserves the original request, justification, approval chain, communications, schedule, dependencies, amendment history, and pre-intervention operational state. No original record may be rewritten, withdrawn, normalized, or replaced in a manner that obscures what had been scheduled.

Duration rule: Each pause carries a recorded expiration condition. Continuing it requires a fresh lawful basis, documented necessity, and renewed authorization from the authority controlling that change. Expiration cannot be avoided through administrative silence.

Substitution rule: An independently necessary change may proceed when its responsible authority authenticates replacement capacity that is genuinely independent, available during the critical window, legally usable, operationally sufficient, and supported by an auditable provenance chain.

Label-independence rule: A different vendor, route, contract, carrier, or organizational owner is not enough. Julie tests whether the proposed replacement still converges on the same fuel, repair, component, cargo, personnel, transportation, or approval dependency.

Julie’s role: Julie reconstructs the combined effect, identifies which changes create the overlap, states proof ceilings, and verifies the provenance and independence of proposed substitutions. She does not issue holds, compel private parties, renegotiate agreements, determine compensation, operate the node, or direct strategic or military policy.

Protective-purpose rule: Authorities may not permit the verified degradation to proceed merely to observe who benefits or reacts. Any investigative monitoring requires separate lawful authorization and cannot override the standstill’s protective purpose.

Proof ceiling: The standstill responds to a verified shared failure point and credible exploitation concern. It does not prove common authorship, coordination, malicious intent, commander involvement, organizational control, state sponsorship, or attempted attack.

Narrative reason: The narrow pause breaks the immediate concurrency window while preserving legitimate commerce, original evidence, separate legal authority, and uncertainty. It also creates a controlled test because a later attempt to recreate the same combined degradation through another administrative route becomes separately assessable evidence.

Canon consequences: Separate authorities retain their own powers and records. Julie remains an independent evidence specialist rather than a regulator or operations commander. The exact issuing authorities, duration, remedies, substitute-capacity criteria, notification language, and first adaptive response remain unresolved.

## Decision 107 — A Pre-Drafted Substitute Package Recreates the Same Concurrency Through Different Channels

Selection: A.

Status: Locked.

Decision: Soon after the Decision 106 standstill notices are issued, a commercially valid substitute package is submitted through different vendors, authorities, contractual provisions, administrative labels, and approval routes.

Functional-equivalence finding: Julie’s cross-system reconstruction establishes that the substitutes would recreate substantially the same combined degradation at the shared commercial fuel-and-repair node during the same critical window, even though the individual requests and labels differ.

The package need not duplicate every original change. Functional equivalence requires authenticated evidence that its combined implementation would restore the material concurrency identified in Decision 105.

Creation-history finding: Preserved versions, repository records, document hashes, signed transmissions, or other lawful source records establish that substantial portions of the substitute package were drafted before the standstill notices were issued.

Inference limit: Pre-drafting supports the bounded inference that some interruption, objection, or alternate route may have been anticipated. It does not prove knowledge of the protected standstill, knowledge of the shared dependency, common direction, malicious intent, or a plan to create an exploitable weakness.

Legitimate explanations remain possible, including ordinary contingency planning, professional-advisor preparation, insurer requirements, parallel vendor planning, shared market assumptions, and independently prepared alternatives.

Legal-status rule: The package is not automatically evidence of a crime, conspiracy, hostile operation, contractual breach, or false statement. Each component retains its own provenance, custodian, legal status, and proof ceiling.

Julie’s authority: Julie may identify functional equivalence, reconstruct document ancestry, isolate common source material, state proof limits, identify unresolved questions, and request preservation through lawful investigative leads. She may not cancel the package, accuse its authors, compel production, direct interviews, or extend commercial controls.

Standstill-extension rule: Decision 106 does not silently expand. A responsible lawful authority must independently verify from authenticated records that a substitute recreates the overlapping degradation before pausing it under the existing time-limited standstill authority.

Any paused substitute receives the same safeguards: ordinary operations continue, original records remain preserved, independently necessary action may proceed with authenticated equivalent capacity, and the pause expires unless lawfully renewed.

Investigative consequence: The substitute package is the first strong indication that the combined operational outcome may matter more than the individual administrative mechanism. It authorizes focused examination of draft ancestry, instructions, common assumptions, intermediaries, distribution history, and cross-system knowledge, subject to each custodian’s lawful authority.

Attribution ceiling: The substitute does not identify the escaped commander, prove reuse of the recurring approval function, establish organizational control or state sponsorship, or show that an attack or disruption was ordered.

Technology rule: The adaptation is caused by human drafting, selection, approval, transmission, and scheduling. No system independently chooses to evade the standstill.

Narrative consequence: The adversarial process demonstrates adaptation without requiring authorities to permit the original vulnerability to deepen. Julie’s function-over-label provenance method produces the breakthrough and preserves multiple innocent explanations until stronger evidence exists.

Open details: The precise package components, vendors, intermediaries, draft custodians, approval authorities, creation chronology, shared sources, preservation mechanisms, and first connecting witness or record remain unresolved.

## Decision 108 — A Common Protected Source-Data Access Path Supplies the Critical Assumptions

Selection: B.

Status: Locked.

Decision: Separate substitute components incorporate the same nonpublic, time-phased dependency extract, including a retired capacity flag and an identical boundary assumption that do not appear in public commercial records.

Authenticated relationship: Canadian and American authorities separately authenticate the protected continuity-planning workspace, source-data custody, authorized-user and group records, access events, export histories, preserved versions, and the copies incorporated into substitute documents within their respective jurisdictions. Those records establish that the relevant drafters or their organizations received the critical extract through one controlled access path before the Decision 106 standstill.

Relationship finding: The common channel supplied the cross-system assumptions needed to recreate substantially the same combined degradation through different vendors, contracts, authorities, and administrative labels. This is the first authenticated evidence of a reusable preparation mechanism behind the Decision 107 substitute package.

Bounded inference: The access path supports focused investigation into selection, permissions, export requests, distribution, incorporation, and cross-system knowledge. It does not prove common drafting, common direction, knowledge of the protected standstill, knowledge of the shared-node dependency, malicious intent, or an effort to create an exploitable weakness.

Legitimate explanations remain possible, including an insurer, commercial consortium, continuity adviser, emergency-planning service, outside professional adviser, or other lawful coordination function. The material may have been used innocently, selectively manipulated, forwarded beyond its intended audience, or exploited by an authorized participant without institutional knowledge.

Credential and identity rule: Access by a credential, group membership, service account, delegated session, or recorded export does not automatically prove which human acted. Possession of the extract does not prove that every drafter saw it, understood the combined degradation, or shared a common purpose.

Jurisdiction and custody: RCMP and the designated United States investigative authority retain separate powers over witnesses, providers, records, preservation, disclosure, and enforcement. Canadian and American repositories remain separate, and no merged evidence pool is created.

Julie’s authority: Julie may identify the common access path, reconstruct source and export chronology, compare document incorporation, state proof limits, identify unresolved questions, and request preservation through lawful investigative leads. She may not compel production, direct interviews, seize systems, suspend access, accuse participants, or extend commercial controls.

Standstill rule: The common access path does not automatically expand or renew the Decision 106 standstill. Each responsible authority must independently determine from authenticated records whether a proposed substitute recreates the protected overlapping degradation before imposing or extending any lawful pause.

Attribution ceiling: The common protected source-data path establishes reusable preparation infrastructure. It does not identify the escaped commander, prove that the recurring approval function controlled the workspace, establish organizational control or state sponsorship, create criminal liability, or show that an attack or disruption was ordered or attempted.

Technology rule: Selection, permissioning, export, transmission, incorporation, and scheduling are human-directed acts. No system independently chooses to coordinate, evade the standstill, or reproduce the concurrency.

Narrative consequence: Julie’s function-over-label and assumption-provenance method produces the breakthrough while preserving legitimate alternatives. The inquiry narrows from diffuse similarity to a controlled access-and-distribution trail without collapsing common preparation infrastructure into common hostile command.

Open details: The workspace’s lawful owner and purpose, exact dataset, source custodians, access-control design, user and group history, export method, recipients, redactions, provider records, cross-border preservation routes, and the first authenticated evidence connecting a recorded access event to a human instruction or decision remain unresolved. The 100,000-word hard minimum and 105,000–112,000-word planning target remain unchanged.

## Decision 109 — A Versioned Human-Authored Extraction Recipe Defines the Critical Selection Logic

Selection: B.

Status: Locked.

Decision: The protected continuity-planning workspace contains a versioned, human-authored extraction recipe: a query definition, field-selection map, exception list, time-window instruction, or equivalent data-selection specification.

Selection-logic finding: The recipe expressly selects the retired capacity flag, preserves the disputed boundary assumption, and excludes or deprioritizes other available records that would have complicated the resulting planning picture. The source records may be authentic; the evidentiary concern lies in the repeatable human instructions that control admission, exclusion, and framing.

Authentication finding: Preserved repository versions establish that the recipe was created and altered through human user credentials or approval roles before the substitute documents were drafted. Query-execution records, export manifests, hashes, signed transmissions, and recipient-side copies establish that the recipe, or an authenticated derivative, generated the critical extracts distributed to multiple organizations.

Role-separation rule: Recipe author, reviser, approver, executor, distributor, recipient, and ultimate beneficiary remain distinct evidentiary roles unless authenticated records connect them. A username, credential, service account, approval role, or execution event does not automatically prove personal action.

Jurisdiction and custody: Canadian and American authorities authenticate records under their separate lawful powers and preserve separate repositories, witnesses, access logs, export histories, custody chains, and disclosure restrictions. No merged evidence pool is created.

Julie’s authority: Julie may correlate certified findings, reconstruct recipe and derivative lineage, identify selected and excluded categories, map approval and execution events, and state proof limits. She may not compel records, direct interviews, accuse a user, attribute intent, merge repositories, or issue operational or commercial controls.

Legitimate explanations remain possible, including standardized continuity planning, insurer or regulatory requirements, inherited risk assumptions, exercise preparation, stale definitions, defensive red-team activity, professional-adviser templates, and authorized reuse without knowledge of the combined downstream effect.

Investigative consequence: The common protected access path is connected to a reusable, human-directed selection mechanism rather than merely to a passive repository. Focused investigation may now distinguish who designed, approved, executed, distributed, and relied upon the recipe while preserving each role as a separate question.

Technology rule: No system independently chose the fields, exclusions, assumptions, recipients, or timing. Human actors created, revised, approved, executed, transmitted, or reused the recipe.

Proof ceiling: The recipe proves a repeatable human-directed selection mechanism and an authenticated path to the distributed extracts. It does not identify the escaped commander, prove that the recurring approval function controlled the recipe, establish common hostile command, malicious intent, organizational control, state sponsorship, criminal liability, knowledge of the combined degradation, or an ordered or attempted attack.

Narrative consequence: Julie’s provenance method produces the breakthrough through version history, selection logic, exclusions, and downstream incorporation. The finding reinforces the series doctrine that authentic records can produce a false operational reality when human decision architecture controls which facts are admitted and emphasized.

Open details: The recipe’s exact technical form, repository, version chronology, selected and excluded fields, approval path, execution account, derivative history, recipients, custodians, and the first authenticated record or witness connecting any two human roles remain unresolved. The 100,000-word hard minimum and 105,000–112,000-word planning target remain unchanged.

## Decision 110 — A Role-Bound Approval Certificate and Nonpublic Decision Rule Connect the Function to the Recipe

Selection: B.

Status: Locked.

Decision: The decisive revision of the versioned extraction recipe carries an authenticated role-bound approval certificate, signed approval object, or equivalent protected authorization associated with the recurring internal approval function.

Version-binding rule: The certificate must bind the approval to the exact recipe-version hash. It identifies an approval function, successor function, or lawfully delegated role rather than conclusively identifying a person. Certificate-chain records, delegation histories, repository versions, approval timestamps, and recipient-side copies must reconcile before the connection is treated as authenticated.

Decision-doctrine finding: The accompanying approval packet applies the same nonpublic exception logic and decision sequence found in the Western Pacific safeguard-removal signature. The match is substantive rather than stylistic. It concerns how conflicting records are categorized, which objections are treated as controlling, what uncertainty is permitted to travel forward, and which safeguards may be omitted from the approved product.

Lineage consequence: The combined evidence establishes that the recurring approval function—or an authenticated successor or authorized delegate—approved the identified recipe version through the same protected decision method. It strengthens procedural and doctrinal lineage without collapsing function, role, credential, delegate, and person into one identity.

Jurisdiction and custody: Canadian and American authorities separately authenticate certificate-chain records, role delegations, approval packets, exact version hashes, repository histories, and local or recipient copies under their own custody and disclosure rules. Classified role identities and compartment records remain controlled by the responsible national authority. The repositories remain separate.

Julie’s authority: Julie may correlate certified findings, reconstruct approval lineage, identify mismatches, preserve competing explanations, and state proof ceilings. She may not compel certificate or personnel records, direct interviews, declassify role identities, accuse a user, or treat a role match as a person match.

Alternative explanations: Legitimate possibilities remain, including inherited doctrine, lawful continuity review, authorized delegation, migration from a retired function, credential misuse, incomplete reviewer awareness, or reuse of the protected decision method for a legitimate purpose.

Proof ceiling: A valid role-bound certificate proves that the identified function, authenticated successor, or authorized delegate approved the exact recipe version. It does not prove which person exercised the role, whether a credential was misused, whether the approver understood the combined degradation, whether the doctrine was inherited for legitimate reasons, whether the escaped commander controlled the approval, or whether malicious intent, common hostile command, organizational control, state sponsorship, criminal liability, or an ordered or attempted attack existed.

Technology rule: The approval, delegation, selection, and transmission remain human-directed. No technological system independently chooses the approval doctrine, the recipe, or the downstream operational effect.

Narrative consequence: The evidence connects the reusable selection mechanism to the recurring approval function through both authenticated authorization and protected decision doctrine while preserving uncertainty about the human actor, purpose, and command chain.

Open details: The certificate format, issuing authority, retired or successor function name, delegation chain, approval-packet structure, exact nonpublic decision rule, classified-role handling, repository custodians, and first evidence capable of distinguishing authorized continuity from misuse or covert direction remain unresolved.

## Decision 111 — An Authenticated Execution-Chain Anomaly Places the Approval Outside the Function’s Normal Operating Path

Selection: B.

Status: Locked.

Decision: The Decision 110 role-bound approval certificate remains valid and continues to bind the recurring approval function, authenticated successor, or authorized delegate to the exact extraction-recipe version. Independently authenticated execution records, however, place the certificate action outside the function’s normal operating chain.

Execution-chain finding: The anomaly may combine a managed endpoint assigned to another compartment or support unit, an unexpected signing-module or hardware-security-module session, a network enclave not ordinarily used for this approval class, a valid multifactor-authentication event separated from the certificate action by an abnormal interval or location, temporary emergency or remote access, or device-custody and secure-room records that do not reconcile with the approval event. The final evidence must identify a concrete departure from the normal chain rather than ordinary administrative variation.

Authentication rule: The anomaly must be established through independently preserved endpoint attestation, signing-service records, certificate timestamps, access and network-path evidence, device-custody records, badge or secure-room records when available, and recipient-side copies. A single dashboard, retrospective summary, or unauthenticated export is insufficient.

Alternative explanations: Emergency continuity routing, a secured shared terminal, authorized technical-administrator assistance, approved remote work, delayed synchronization, credential compromise, deliberate proxy use, and covert direction remain viable until authenticated evidence excludes them.

Jurisdiction and custody: The lawful authority controlling each endpoint, signing service, access system, or physical-security record authenticates and retains its own evidence. Canadian and United States investigative authorities preserve separate repositories, witnesses, custody chains, and disclosure rules. No merged evidence pool is created.

Julie’s authority: Julie may correlate certified findings, reconstruct the execution chronology, identify conflicts among authentication, device, network, and physical-access records, preserve competing explanations, and state proof ceilings. She may not seize endpoints, compel personnel records, direct internal-security interviews, identify a credential user through inference, declassify role identities, or treat abnormal execution as proof of misuse.

Proof ceiling: The anomalous execution path narrows ordinary explanations and proves that a valid role-bound approval was exercised through circumstances requiring investigation. It does not prove that a credential was misused, identify the natural person who acted, establish that the credential holder was present or absent, prove malicious intent, show that the escaped commander controlled the event, or establish common hostile command, organizational control, state sponsorship, criminal liability, or an ordered or attempted attack.

Technology rule: The credential, endpoint, signing service, authentication system, network path, and continuity mechanisms are non-sentient. Human actors configure, approve, initiate, assist, misuse, conceal, or investigate their use.

Narrative consequence: The evidence creates the first concrete divide between formally valid authorization and the circumstances of execution. Julie’s breakthrough comes from reconciling individually authentic but mutually incomplete records while the responsible person, purpose, and command chain remain unresolved.

Open details: The exact anomaly combination, normal approval path, endpoint and signing-service custodians, emergency-access rules, physical-access evidence, timing offsets, redactions or denied records, first lawful witness inquiry, and later evidence distinguishing authorized continuity from credential misuse, proxy action, or covert direction remain unresolved. The 100,000-word hard minimum and 105,000–112,000-word planning target remain unchanged.

## Decision 112 — A Just-in-Time Access-Enablement Record Connects an Authorized Support Role to the Anomalous Session

Selection: B.

Status: Locked.

Decision: A preserved service ticket, signed access request, temporary-entitlement object, emergency-access authorization, session-broker record, or equivalent administrative artifact establishes that a second protected role enabled the anomalous endpoint or signing session shortly before the extraction-recipe approval.

Version and session binding: The enablement record connects to the exact recipe-version hash or approval object, the anomalous managed endpoint or signing module, a narrowly defined activation window, and the temporary privilege, routing change, device association, or continuity exception that made the certificate action possible. It predates the approval event and reconciles with signing-service chronology, endpoint attestation, multifactor-authentication evidence, network-path records, and recipient-side copies.

Role-separation rule: Requester, access approver, technical implementer, role-credential holder, session operator, recipe approver, distributor, recipient, and ultimate beneficiary remain separate evidentiary roles unless authenticated records connect them. A valid enablement by one role does not identify the person who later exercised the approval credential or establish the enabling actor’s knowledge or intent.

Authentication rule: The finding must rest on native access-management records, preserved versions, authenticated approval history, entitlement and revocation events, privileged-session or broker logs, endpoint and signing-service records, and available communications or recipient-side evidence. A retrospective ticket summary, screenshot, or unsupported administrator recollection is insufficient.

Alternative explanations: Emergency continuity support, approved remote work, secured shared-terminal use, routine administrator assistance, lawful deadline recovery, temporary staff substitution, credential compromise, deliberate proxy use, and covert direction remain viable until authenticated evidence excludes them.

Jurisdiction and custody: The authority controlling the access-management system authenticates and retains the enablement record. Endpoint, signing-service, personnel, physical-security, and recipient records remain with separate lawful custodians. Canadian and United States investigative authorities retain separate repositories, witnesses, custody chains, and disclosure rules. No merged evidence pool is created.

Julie’s authority: Julie may reconstruct the request, approval, implementation, activation, use, and revocation chronology; distinguish enabling action from certificate execution; identify conflicts among role, device, network, and physical-access records; preserve alternatives; and state proof ceilings. She may not compel personnel records, direct internal-security interviews, identify a user through inference, suspend accounts, declassify role identities, or treat the support role as the approval actor.

Proof ceiling: The authenticated access-enablement record proves that a human-authorized administrative action created the conditions for the anomalous approval session. It does not prove that the requester, approver, or implementer executed the certificate action, understood the extraction recipe’s combined effect, acted outside lawful authority, shared hostile purpose, received covert direction, or had any relationship to the escaped commander. It does not establish common hostile command, organizational control, state sponsorship, criminal liability, or an ordered or attempted attack.

Technology rule: Access-management systems, privileged-session brokers, endpoints, signing services, authentication mechanisms, and continuity controls are non-sentient. Human actors request, approve, implement, use, revoke, conceal, or investigate access.

Narrative consequence: The evidence creates the first authenticated human enabling step between the valid role-bound certificate and anomalous execution. Julie gains a concrete administrative provenance trail while opportunity, authorization, execution, knowledge, intent, and command remain separate questions.

Open details: The exact access artifact, protected support role, requester and approval roles, entitlement type, activation and revocation times, endpoint or signing-module relationship, emergency-access basis, redactions or denied records, first lawful witness inquiry, and later evidence distinguishing innocent continuity assistance from credential misuse, authorized proxy action, or covert direction remain unresolved. The 100,000-word hard minimum and 105,000–112,000-word planning target remain unchanged.

## Decision 113 — An Authenticated Protected Tasking Object Directs the Exact Access Enablement

A human-controlled process specifically directed creation of the route used for the exact approval action. This proves direction of the enabling step, not the legality, purpose, understanding, or ultimate command behind it.

Role-separation rule: Tasking author, authorizing role, routing or transmitting role, recipient, access requester, access approver, technical implementer, credential holder, session operator, recipe approver, and ultimate beneficiary remain distinct evidentiary roles unless authenticated evidence connects them. A protected role, account, signature, or routing entry does not automatically identify a natural person.

The tasking may have been lawful within a narrow administrative purpose even if the downstream use was not understood.
