No merged evidence pool is created.

Julie’s authority: Julie may correlate certified findings, reconstruct the tasking-to-enablement-to-execution chronology, distinguish roles, identify conflicts and missing records, preserve competing explanations, and state proof ceilings. She may not compel tasking or personnel records, direct internal-security interviews, identify a natural person from role metadata alone, declassify protected instructions, accuse a participant, or treat specific direction as proof of hostile command.

Proof ceiling: The authenticated tasking object proves that a human-controlled process specifically directed creation of the temporary access route used for the anomalous approval. It does not necessarily identify the tasking’s natural-person author, prove that the instruction was unlawful, show that recipients understood the extraction recipe’s combined effect, identify the session operator or certificate actor, establish malicious intent, connect the direction to the escaped commander, or prove common hostile command, organizational control, state sponsorship, criminal liability, or an ordered or attempted attack.

Technology rule: The tasking, routing, enablement, authentication, session, and approval mechanisms are non-sentient. Human actors create, authorize, transmit, implement, interpret, misuse, conceal, or investigate them.

Narrative consequence: The inquiry advances from an anomalous approval and a human-authorized enabling action to authenticated specific direction of that enabling action. Julie gains a defensible human-control chain without collapsing the directing role, implementer, session operator, approver, and commander into one identity.

Open details: The tasking system, document form, exact directive language, protected roles, authentication controls, activation window, routing path, recipients, acknowledgments, redactions, privilege or classification disputes, first evidence connecting the tasking to an originating authority, and later evidence distinguishing lawful administrative direction from deceptive sponsorship or covert operational purpose remain unresolved. ## Decision 114 — An Embedded Parent-Request Hash Connects the Tasking to a Protected Sponsor Package

Selection: B.

Status: Locked.

Decision: The protected tasking established in Decision 113 contains an immutable parent-request identifier, object hash, signed reference, or equivalent provenance marker. That marker resolves to a preserved parent request held in a separate authoritative repository. The parent package identifies the protected sponsoring function, defines the administrative objective and requested support, names or categorizes the relevant systems and roles, records the requested activation period, carries a role-bound sponsor authorization or delegated approval, predates the child tasking and access enablement, and is cryptographically or documentarily bound to the exact tasking issued to the support role.

Authentication finding: The parent request, child tasking, access-enablement record, anomalous session, role-bound certificate action, and exact extraction-recipe version form an authenticated document ancestry. Each link must be supported by preserved hashes, identifiers, signatures, version history, routing or transmission records, repository metadata, recipient-side copies, and chronology appropriate to that link. A visible reference or matching label alone is insufficient.

Sponsoring-function finding: The verified parent-child relationship establishes that a protected sponsoring function originated or authorized the tasking chain. It proves institutional sponsorship of the defined administrative request, not the sponsor’s full understanding, honesty of purpose, downstream control, or natural-person identity.

Role-separation rule: Sponsoring function, parent-package author, role-bound approver, delegate, transmitting role, tasking recipient, access requester, access approver, technical implementer, credential holder, session operator, recipe approver, downstream drafter, and ultimate beneficiary remain separate evidentiary roles unless authenticated evidence connects them. Institutional sponsorship is not personal attribution.

Stated-purpose rule: The parent package may state a facially legitimate purpose such as continuity support, compartmented recovery, protected review, deadline assurance, security segregation, resilience testing, or technical restoration. That purpose is an observed record, not proof that it was truthful or deceptive. Legitimate, incomplete, misunderstood, inherited, and deceptively framed explanations remain possible.

Jurisdiction and custody: The parent package may reside in a different protected repository from the tasking, access, endpoint, signing, recipient, or downstream records. Each lawful custodian authenticates and retains its own evidence. Canadian and United States authorities preserve separate repositories, witness authority, privileges, classification controls, disclosure rules, and custody chains. No universal evidence pool is created.

Julie’s authority: Julie may correlate certified hashes and identifiers, reconstruct the sponsor-to-parent-to-tasking-to-enablement-to-execution ancestry, distinguish observed records from inferred relationships, identify broken links and missing records, preserve alternative explanations, and state bounded conclusions. She may not compel the parent repository, identify a natural person from a role certificate, declassify protected sponsor material, direct interviews, accuse the sponsoring function, merge national holdings, or treat sponsorship as proof of hostile purpose.

Proof ceiling: The authenticated parent-child relationship proves that a protected sponsoring function originated or authorized the tasking chain and binds that sponsor package to the exact enablement and approval ancestry. It does not prove that the sponsor understood the extraction recipe’s combined effect, that the stated objective was false, that the natural-person author or approver acted maliciously, that the sponsor controlled downstream drafting or implementation, that the escaped commander directed or controlled the sponsor, or that common hostile command, organizational control, state sponsorship, criminal liability, or an ordered or attempted attack existed.

Technology rule: Hashing, signing, routing, repository, access, session, and approval systems are non-sentient. Human actors create, authorize, transmit, implement, interpret, misuse, conceal, preserve, and investigate the records and actions.

Narrative consequence: Julie can now prove an authenticated source-to-effect provenance chain from a protected sponsoring function through the parent request, child tasking, access enablement, anomalous execution route, role-bound approval, and exact extraction-recipe version. The central uncertainty shifts from where the tasking originated to whether the sponsor’s stated administrative objective was genuine, incomplete, deceptive, or supplied by another human authority.

Open details: The sponsoring function’s institutional category, parent repository, objective language, parent-request form, authorization chain, delegate scope, named or categorized systems, activation period, redactions, privileges, classification barriers, recipients, missing attachments, first evidence testing the truthfulness of the stated objective, and later evidence connecting sponsorship to knowledge, deceptive purpose, or a higher directing authority remain unresolved. The 100,000-word hard minimum and 105,000–112,000-word planning target remain unchanged.

## Decision 115 — A Versioned Sponsor-Side Objective Matrix Defines the Unstated Operational Success Criteria

Selection: B.

Status: Locked.

Decision: The parent repository contains a versioned decision matrix, outcome map, scenario-control sheet, protected annex, or equivalent human-authored planning object associated with the sponsor package established in Decision 114. The matrix defines measurable success criteria corresponding to the combined degradation later recreated through the substitute package. The criteria include the same critical time window, the same protected dependency assumptions, preservation of the retired capacity flag, retention of the disputed boundary assumption, exclusion or deprioritization of resilience records, acceptable degradation thresholds across separate commercial systems, and a requirement that the result remain materially stable when vendors, authorities, labels, or contractual routes are substituted.

Authentication finding: The matrix is authenticated through native version history, parent-request references, hashes, role-bound approvals or review records, access history, repository metadata, preserved copies, and chronology. It predates the child tasking, access enablement, anomalous approval session, and downstream substitute drafting. Functional correspondence must be demonstrated through comparison of the matrix criteria, recipe logic, exported assumptions, substitute-package provisions, and observed effects rather than through a conclusory label.

Knowledge finding: The matrix proves that sponsor-side human actors defined, reviewed, or approved measurable criteria corresponding to the combined degradation. This is the first authenticated sponsor-side evidence of knowledge about the result being produced rather than merely knowledge of the administrative process, access route, or approval ancestry.

Stated-objective comparison: The facially benign stated objective and the matrix’s internal success criteria remain separate observed records. Alignment may support legitimate resilience testing, adversarial planning, continuity benchmarking, insurance modeling, contingency analysis, or a compartmented scenario. Divergence may support an incomplete, misunderstood, inherited, or deceptively framed purpose. The matrix does not resolve that question by itself.

Role-separation rule: Sponsoring function, matrix author, editor, reviewer, role-bound approver, parent-package author, delegate, tasking recipient, access implementer, credential holder, session operator, recipe approver, downstream drafter, and ultimate beneficiary remain separate evidentiary roles unless authenticated evidence connects them. Repository access, role metadata, approval status, and document authorship do not automatically identify a natural person’s knowledge or intent.

Jurisdiction and custody: Each lawful custodian authenticates and retains its own matrix versions, parent records, access history, approvals, tasking records, endpoint evidence, recipient copies, and downstream materials. Canadian and United States authorities preserve separate repositories, witnesses, privileges, classification controls, disclosure rules, and custody chains. No universal evidence pool is created.

Julie’s authority: Julie may compare the stated objective with the matrix’s measurable criteria, reconstruct version and approval history, correlate the criteria with recipe logic and downstream effects, distinguish observed facts from inferred knowledge, identify missing links, preserve legitimate explanations, and state bounded findings. She may not declare the planning object unlawful, infer a natural-person author from role metadata, compel protected records, direct sponsor-side interviews, declassify the annex, accuse the sponsoring function, or convert functional correspondence into commander or state attribution.

Proof ceiling: The authenticated objective matrix proves that sponsor-side human actors defined, reviewed, or approved criteria corresponding to the combined degradation and that those criteria predated the tasking and downstream substitute drafting. It does not prove that the criteria were malicious, that the sponsor’s stated objective was knowingly false, that every sponsor-side or downstream actor understood the full purpose, that a particular natural person possessed the relevant knowledge, that the sponsor controlled implementation, that the escaped commander directed or controlled the sponsor, or that common hostile command, organizational control, state sponsorship, criminal liability, or an ordered or attempted attack existed.

Technology rule: The planning, comparison, repository, access, routing, extraction, and approval systems are non-sentient. Human actors define criteria, select assumptions, authorize work, interpret results, transmit records, implement decisions, conceal facts, preserve evidence, and investigate the chain.

Narrative consequence: Julie can now prove that the sponsoring side possessed a human-authored, measurable definition of the result later recreated across substitute commercial routes. The central uncertainty advances from whether the sponsor knew the relevant outcome to why those criteria were chosen, how their purpose was represented internally, and which human authority first connected them to the downstream operational package.

Open details: The matrix’s exact form, version history, authorship roles, reviewers, approvers, terminology, thresholds, excluded resilience records, legitimate planning context, relationship to the stated objective, redactions, missing annexes, first downstream recipient, first documented objection, and first authenticated evidence showing whether sponsor-side actors intended the criteria to remain concealed, deniable, or operationally effective remain unresolved. The 100,000-word hard minimum and 105,000–112,000-word planning target remain unchanged.

## Decision 116 — An Authenticated Requirements Crosswalk Maps Every Protected Criterion to a Benign Administrative Label and Downstream Provision

Selection: B.

Status: Locked.

Decision: The sponsor repository contains a versioned requirements crosswalk, translation table, implementation map, traceability record, or equivalent human-authored planning object. It connects each protected objective-matrix criterion established in Decision 115 to a neutral administrative or commercial label, a child-tasking requirement, an extraction-recipe field or exception, a substitute-package provision, and the resulting measurable degradation effect.

Crosswalk content: The mapped relationships include, as applicable, a degradation threshold presented as a continuity tolerance; the critical window presented as an ordinary service or implementation period; the retired capacity flag presented as a validated planning baseline; the disputed boundary assumption presented as an accepted interoperability rule; exclusion or deprioritization of resilience records presented as scope control, dataset normalization, or conflict resolution; and survival across substituted vendors, authorities, labels, or contractual routes presented as implementation flexibility.

Authentication finding: The crosswalk predates final child tasking, access enablement, extraction-recipe approval, and downstream substitute drafting. It is authenticated through native version history, hashes, parent-request references, approval records, access history, routing or transmission records, recipient-side copies, and correspondence between its mapped fields and the separately preserved downstream records. Functional correspondence must be demonstrated criterion by criterion; similar vocabulary or general thematic resemblance is insufficient.

Deliberate-translation finding: Sponsor-side human actors systematically translated protected success criteria into outwardly different implementation language while preserving their operative function. The objective matrix was not merely inherited by coincidence or reproduced through independent drafting. The crosswalk establishes a human-controlled semantic and functional ancestry from the sponsor’s internal criteria to the tasking, extraction recipe, substitute provisions, and combined degradation.

Role-separation rule: Matrix author, crosswalk author, translator, reviewer, role-bound approver, parent-package sponsor, tasking author, tasking recipient, access implementer, session operator, recipe approver, downstream drafter, contracting authority, vendor, and ultimate beneficiary remain separate evidentiary roles unless authenticated evidence connects them. A mapped requirement does not prove that every recipient understood its protected origin or combined effect.

Alternative explanations: Lawful compartmentation, security sanitization, procurement translation, compatibility mapping, standardized traceability, protection of classified or proprietary methods, resilience testing, insurance modeling, adversarial planning, and ordinary conversion of technical criteria into implementation language remain possible. Translation and preserved function do not alone establish deception or illegality.

Jurisdiction and custody: Each lawful custodian authenticates and retains its own crosswalk, parent package, tasking, access, endpoint, signing, recipe, commercial, and downstream records. Canadian and United States authorities preserve separate repositories, legal authorities, witness processes, privileges, classification controls, disclosure rules, and custody chains. No universal evidence pool is created.

Julie’s authority: Julie may reconstruct criterion-level traceability, compare hashes and versions, distinguish semantic labels from functional effects, identify missing or broken links, test alternative explanations, and state bounded conclusions. She may not compel protected crosswalks or personnel records, declassify sponsor material, direct interviews, identify a natural person from role metadata, accuse the sponsor or translators, determine legality, or merge national repositories.

Proof ceiling: The authenticated requirements crosswalk proves that sponsor-side human actors deliberately mapped protected objective criteria into outwardly different administrative or commercial language while preserving their function through the child tasking, extraction recipe, substitute-package provisions, and resulting combined degradation. It does not prove that the translation was unlawful or deceptive, that the benign labels were knowingly false, that every recipient understood the protected objective or combined effect, that a particular natural person possessed malicious intent, that the sponsor controlled every downstream action, that the escaped commander directed or controlled the process, or that common hostile command, organizational control, state sponsorship, criminal liability, or an ordered or attempted attack existed.

Technology rule: The crosswalk, traceability, document, routing, access, signing, and approval systems are non-sentient. Human actors define, translate, authorize, transmit, implement, interpret, misuse, conceal, preserve, and investigate the requirements and records.

Narrative consequence: Julie can now demonstrate deliberate semantic translation and preserved functional ancestry from sponsor-side success criteria to the combined degradation despite changed terminology, vendors, authorities, and contractual routes. The central uncertainty shifts to whether the translation was ordinary protected implementation work or was designed to prevent any downstream reviewer, oversight body, or affected authority from seeing the combined effect.

Open details: The crosswalk’s form, criterion identifiers, neutral labels, implementation fields, authorship and review roles, version sequence, approval chain, distribution controls, redactions, compartment boundaries, recipient visibility, missing mappings, first evidence that review was deliberately fragmented, and later evidence distinguishing lawful compartmentation from deceptive concealment or covert operational purpose remain unresolved. The 100,000-word hard minimum and 105,000–112,000-word planning target remain unchanged.

## Decision 117 — A Versioned Review-Allocation Matrix Deliberately Splits the Crosswalk Across Separate Approval Channels

Selection: B.

Status: Locked.

Decision: The sponsor repository contains a versioned review-allocation matrix, compartment plan, approval-routing map, or equivalent human-authored control object. It assigns the translated requirements established in Decision 116 across separate specialist review teams, commercial or technical packages, vendors and contracting paths, approval queues, legal authorities, national repositories, activation periods, or implementation stages.

Visibility design: Each downstream reviewer receives only the locally framed requirement and supporting rationale needed for that review path. The master requirements crosswalk remains restricted to a sponsor-side integration role. No downstream package contains enough information to reconstruct the combined degradation, and no single downstream reviewer has access before approval to all critical thresholds, dependency assumptions, exclusions, timing relationships, and substitution rules. The protected criteria are recombined only after their separate review paths have substantially completed.

Authentication finding: The fragmentation design and actual reviewer visibility are authenticated through native version history, hashes, access-control records, package-generation records, distribution histories, reviewer assignments, approval timestamps, acknowledgment records, recipient-side copies, and preserved relationships to the parent request, crosswalk, child tasking, extraction recipe, and substitute provisions. The assigned packages and access records must show that the planned visibility boundaries were implemented.

Controlled-fragmentation finding: Sponsor-side human actors designed and executed a review architecture that preserved the integrated view within a restricted sponsor-side role while distributing only local fragments through downstream approval channels. The obscured combined effect resulted from a human-controlled review design rather than accidental bureaucratic separation, independent drafting, or merely incompatible repositories.

Role-separation rule: Review-allocation author, compartment designer, integration-role holder, package generator, access administrator, reviewer, approval-queue owner, contracting authority, vendor, tasking author, access implementer, recipe approver, downstream drafter, and ultimate beneficiary remain separate evidentiary roles unless authenticated evidence connects them. Limited visibility does not prove that a downstream participant was deceived, complicit, negligent, or aware of the combined effect.

Alternative explanations: Lawful compartmentation, national need-to-know rules, privilege, commercial confidentiality, source protection, conflict-of-interest separation, technical specialization, procurement segregation, classified-method protection, phased implementation, and ordinary allocation of complex reviews remain possible. Deliberate fragmentation and sponsor-side retention of the integrated view do not alone establish an intent to evade lawful scrutiny.

Jurisdiction and custody: Each lawful custodian authenticates and retains its own review-allocation records, access controls, packages, reviewer assignments, approvals, commercial records, and downstream evidence. Canadian and United States authorities preserve separate repositories, legal authorities, witness processes, privileges, classification controls, disclosure rules, and custody chains. No universal evidence pool or cross-border command authority is created.

Julie’s authority: Julie may map what each role and reviewer could access at each time, compare package hashes and approval chronology, test whether stated compartment boundaries match actual distribution, identify missing integrated reviews, preserve legitimate explanations, and state bounded conclusions. She may not compel protected allocation records or personnel files, declassify sponsor material, direct interviews, identify natural persons from role metadata, accuse reviewers or sponsors, determine legality, or merge national repositories.

Proof ceiling: The authenticated review-allocation matrix proves deliberate fragmentation of the translated requirements across separate approval channels and sponsor-side retention of the integrated view. It does not prove that the purpose was to evade lawful review, that any particular reviewer would have objected if given the complete picture, that downstream participants were knowingly misled or complicit, that the fragmentation was unlawful, that a particular natural person possessed malicious intent, that the sponsor controlled every downstream action, that the escaped commander designed or controlled the architecture, or that common hostile command, organizational control, state sponsorship, criminal liability, or an ordered or attempted attack existed.

Technology rule: Routing, access-control, package-generation, repository, acknowledgment, and approval systems are non-sentient. Human actors design, authorize, configure, use, interpret, misuse, conceal, preserve, and investigate the review architecture and its records.

Narrative consequence: Julie can now prove not only that protected criteria were translated, but that their downstream review was intentionally divided so the sponsor-side integration role retained the only complete view. The central uncertainty shifts to whether the restricted integrated view served a legitimate compartmentation need or was deliberately withheld until separate approvals made meaningful integrated review impracticable.

Open details: The allocation matrix’s form, integration role, review teams, package boundaries, access rules, national and legal compartments, approval sequence, timing of recombination, missing acknowledgments, redactions, policy justifications, first evidence testing whether integrated review was intentionally delayed or prevented, and later evidence connecting the architecture to deceptive purpose or a higher directing authority remain unresolved. The 100,000-word hard minimum and 105,000–112,000-word planning target remain unchanged.

## Decision 118 — A Versioned Release-Gate Instruction Withholds the Integrated Crosswalk Until Local Approvals Can No Longer Meaningfully Change the Outcome

Selection: B.

Status: Locked.

Decision: The deliberate review fragmentation established in Decision 117 is governed by a separately preserved sponsor-side release-gate instruction, control document, access rule, or equivalent human-authored planning object. The gate controls the master requirements crosswalk and the integrated-effect analysis that would allow a reviewer to reconstruct the combined degradation.

Release conditions: The integrated view remains unavailable to downstream reviewers until a defined combination of local technical and commercial approvals, contracting commitments, vendor-capacity reservations, regulatory or administrative windows, temporary-access and certificate actions, and implementation stages has been completed or has passed its ordinary suspension point. The gate is tied to milestones that make reopening the separately approved components materially difficult, costly, delayed, jurisdictionally complex, or disclosure-sensitive.

Late-review limitation: When the integrated crosswalk is released, the consolidated review is limited to informational, monitoring, implementation-support, lessons-learned, assurance, or post-commitment functions. The late-stage reviewer lacks ordinary authority to suspend, reopen, reverse, or require renewed approval of the separately completed components. Formal visibility therefore arrives after the last ordinary point at which it could perform the normal function of preapproval review.

Authentication finding: The release gate and its operation are authenticated through native version history, sponsor authorization, access-control rules, package-release records, approval timestamps, contracting and capacity chronology, certificate and activation events, recipient-side copies, and the timing of the first integrated review. The finding requires a record-by-record comparison between when complete visibility became possible and when meaningful intervention authority expired. A later document’s existence alone is insufficient.

Operational-ineffectiveness finding: Sponsor-side human actors deliberately scheduled integrated visibility for a stage at which the separate approvals and commitments were substantially fixed. The architecture did not merely restrict access to the combined view; it delayed that view until the designated consolidated review could not ordinarily alter the outcome.

Role-separation rule: Release-gate author, sponsor-side approver, access-control custodian, crosswalk custodian, local reviewer, contracting authority, vendor, access implementer, session operator, recipe approver, downstream drafter, late-stage integration reviewer, and ultimate beneficiary remain distinct evidentiary roles unless authenticated evidence connects them. Formal access does not establish practical authority, personal knowledge, or agreement with the result.

Alternative explanations: Lawful source protection, compartment integrity, transaction certainty, emergency continuity, deadline management, protection of classified or proprietary methods, avoidance of duplicative review, national need-to-know rules, and a good-faith decision not to reopen local approvals remain possible. Timing is probative only when compared with the authority and intervention options available at each stage.

Jurisdiction and custody: Each lawful custodian authenticates and retains its own release-gate, access, approval, contracting, certificate, implementation, and reviewer-authority records. Canadian and United States authorities preserve separate repositories, legal powers, witness processes, privileges, classification controls, disclosure rules, and custody chains. No universal evidence pool is created.

Julie’s authority: Julie may reconstruct the visibility-and-authority chronology, distinguish formal availability from meaningful intervention power, compare certified release and approval events, identify missing or conflicting records, test legitimate explanations, and state bounded conclusions. She may not compel protected sponsor records, declassify gate instructions, direct interviews, identify a natural person from role metadata, decide legality, accuse participants, reopen approvals, or merge national repositories.

Proof ceiling: The authenticated release gate proves that sponsor-side actors deliberately withheld the integrated view until the separate approvals and commitments were substantially fixed, and that the later consolidated review lacked ordinary authority to suspend, reopen, or reverse the outcome. It does not prove that the delay was unlawful or deceptive, that earlier integrated review would have stopped the package, that every sponsor-side actor understood the combined degradation, that downstream reviewers were knowingly misled, that a particular natural person possessed malicious intent, that the sponsor controlled every downstream action, that the escaped commander designed or controlled the gate, or that common hostile command, organizational control, state sponsorship, criminal liability, or an ordered or attempted attack existed.

Technology rule: Access-control, routing, release, signing, contracting, and review systems are non-sentient. Human actors design, authorize, enforce, bypass, interpret, misuse, conceal, preserve, and investigate the gates, records, and decisions.

Narrative consequence: Julie can now prove that an integrated review formally existed but was deliberately made operationally ineffective by timing and lack of intervention authority. The central uncertainty shifts from whether the sponsor retained the complete view to why the sponsor ensured that no empowered reviewer received it before the outcome became difficult to change.

Open details: The release-gate form, milestone sequence, precise intervention cutoff, sponsor authorization, access custodians, affected packages, contracting and capacity commitments, late-review mandate, redactions, privileges, classification barriers, first evidence that the gate was adopted or revised in response to an earlier intervention risk, and later evidence connecting the timing design to deceptive purpose or a higher directing authority remain unresolved. The 100,000-word hard minimum and 105,000–112,000-word planning target remain unchanged.

## Decision 119 — A Versioned Intervention-Authority Map Aligns Every Release Condition with the Loss of a Reviewer’s Power to Act

Selection: B.

Status: Locked.

Decision: The sponsor repository contains a versioned intervention-authority map, reviewer-power matrix, finality schedule, or equivalent human-authored control object. For every relevant review channel, it records what information the reviewer can see; the legal, contractual, technical, safety, regulatory, procurement, administrative, or national-security authority the reviewer possesses; the conditions under which the reviewer may suspend, reopen, escalate, condition, or reject a component; the deadline or milestone after which that authority expires or becomes practically ineffective; and the corresponding release condition governing the master requirements crosswalk and integrated-effect analysis.

Authority-alignment finding: The map shows that integrated visibility remains withheld until each potentially effective reviewer has either completed a local review without the combined picture, lost ordinary authority to reopen the matter, passed a statutory, contractual, procurement, regulatory, or administrative deadline, or become unable to intervene without exceptional senior action, material cost, disclosure, or cross-jurisdictional disruption. The late-stage integration role is separately identified as informational or advisory and lacks ordinary power to suspend, reopen, condition, or reverse the separately approved components.

Authentication finding: The map predates the relevant release-gate operation and is authenticated through native version history, sponsor authorization, reviewer charters, delegation records, access-control rules, release-gate configuration, package hashes, local approval timestamps, contracting and capacity milestones, recipient-side copies, and the actual chronology of when each reviewer could see and act. A general finality schedule or later reconstruction is insufficient; the record must bind named or defined review powers to the release conditions actually used.

Deliberate-alignment finding: The gate’s milestones were selected by reference to the point at which identifiable reviewers ceased to possess meaningful intervention power, not merely because ordinary project events happened to occur then. Formal authority and practically exercisable authority remain distinct.

Role-separation rule: Intervention-map author, reviewer-power analyst, sponsor-side approver, release-gate designer, access custodian, local reviewer, late-stage integration reviewer, contracting official, vendor, implementation actor, and ultimate beneficiary remain distinct evidentiary roles unless authenticated evidence connects them. Institutional knowledge of reviewer authority is not natural-person attribution, and limited reviewer visibility is not proof of reviewer complicity.

Alternative explanations: Lawful coordination among overlapping authorities, prevention of inconsistent decisions, protection of national compartments, transaction certainty, ordinary finality management, emergency continuity, privilege, source protection, commercial confidentiality, and avoidance of duplicative review remain possible. Mapping intervention authority and managing finality are not inherently improper.

Jurisdiction and custody: Each lawful custodian authenticates and retains its own reviewer mandate, delegation, local package, access rights, approval record, finality rule, contract, release-gate record, and recipient copy. Canadian and United States authorities preserve separate repositories, legal powers, witness processes, privileges, classification controls, disclosure rules, and custody chains. No universal evidence pool is created.

Julie’s authority: Julie may reconstruct what each reviewer could see, what authority each possessed, when that authority expired or became impracticable, and how those points corresponded to release conditions. She may distinguish formal availability from timely intervention power, test alternative explanations, identify missing or inconsistent records, and state bounded conclusions. She may not compel protected mandates or personnel records, determine legality, infer a natural person from role metadata, direct interviews, accuse the sponsor or reviewers, merge national repositories, or treat authority alignment as proof of hostile command.

Proof ceiling: The authenticated intervention-authority map proves that sponsor-side human actors knew which reviewers could meaningfully intervene and deliberately aligned integrated disclosure with the expiration or practical loss of those powers. It does not prove that the alignment was unlawful, that any reviewer would have objected or stopped the package, that the purpose was deception, that every participant understood the combined degradation, that a particular natural person possessed malicious intent, that the sponsor controlled every downstream action, that the escaped commander designed or controlled the map or gate, or that common hostile command, organizational control, state sponsorship, criminal liability, or an ordered or attempted attack existed.

Technology rule: The mapping, routing, access-control, release-gate, approval, contracting, and review systems are non-sentient. Human actors define authority, select milestones, authorize release conditions, implement access rules, interpret records, preserve evidence, misuse processes, conceal conduct, or investigate them.

Narrative consequence: Julie can now establish that the integrated view was released not merely after ordinary milestones, but after each reviewer capable of changing the result had lost the ordinary ability to do so. The central uncertainty shifts to whether sponsor-side actors also anticipated the specific objection, hold, safeguard, or escalation that an effective integrated review would have produced.

Open details: The map’s form, review channels, authority categories, reviewer charters, expiration and impracticability rules, release conditions, exceptional-action thresholds, national and commercial boundaries, version sequence, approving roles, redactions, first evidence of an anticipated or actual reviewer response, and later evidence connecting process design to deceptive purpose or a higher directing authority remain unresolved. The 100,000-word hard minimum and 105,000–112,000-word planning target remain unchanged.

## Decision 120 — A Versioned Counterfactual Review-Response Analysis Forecasts the Exact Holds and Safeguards Early Disclosure Would Trigger

Selection: B.

Status: Locked.

Decision: The sponsor repository contains a versioned counterfactual assessment, intervention-risk analysis, review-consequence matrix, decision-tree model, or equivalent human-authored planning object. For each potentially effective review channel, it identifies the integrated information that would become visible, the rule, concern, or threshold the reviewer would likely invoke, the reviewer’s available intervention power, the expected hold, safeguard, escalation, consultation, disclosure, or reopening action, the likely duration and effect on contracts, capacity reservations, approvals, or implementation windows, the milestone after which the predicted action would become unavailable or materially harder to exercise, and the corresponding release-gate condition.

Forecasted interventions: The analysis may forecast restoration of excluded resilience records; rejection or qualification of the retired capacity flag; qualification of the disputed boundary assumption; reopening of substitute contracts or local approvals; suspension of the approval certificate; additional cross-system safety or resilience review; renewed statutory or contractual consultation; or referral of the combined package for senior or cross-national review. A general reference to delay, scrutiny, or risk is insufficient. The forecast must identify a reviewer, an intervention power, a predicted action, and the gate milestone associated with the predicted loss of that action.

Authentication finding: The assessment predates final operation of the release gate. Native version history establishes that predicted reviewer responses and the gate conditions were developed, compared, or revised together. Authentication rests on hashes, parent-package and requirements-crosswalk references, intervention-authority-map identifiers, sponsor-side approvals, access and distribution records, preserved comments or resolution entries, and correspondence between forecasted intervention points and the release conditions actually adopted.

Prospective-knowledge finding: Sponsor-side human actors did not merely know when review authority would expire. They prospectively considered what particular effective reviewers were expected to do if given the integrated picture in time and related those predicted interventions to the timing of disclosure. This establishes foresight concerning specific review consequences, not forecast accuracy, gate lawfulness, or purpose.

Role-separation rule: Assessment author, reviewer-power analyst, legal or operational adviser, sponsor-side reviewer, role-bound approver, release-gate designer, access custodian, local reviewer, contracting official, implementation actor, downstream recipient, and ultimate beneficiary remain separate evidentiary roles unless authenticated evidence connects them. A forecast attributed to a role does not automatically establish which natural person wrote, approved, received, or understood it.

Alternative explanations: Ordinary compliance planning, legal contingency analysis, schedule-risk management, transaction management, red-team work, resilience planning, procurement coordination, protection of sensitive methods, and preparation for lawful reviewer demands remain possible. Forecasting possible holds and safeguards is not inherently improper.

Jurisdiction and custody: The assessment may contain privileged legal analysis, classified operational judgments, protected procurement advice, or comparisons among national authorities. Each lawful custodian authenticates and controls its own version, access history, approvals, and related records. Canadian and United States authorities preserve separate repositories, legal powers, privileges, classification controls, disclosure rules, witness processes, and custody chains. No universal evidence pool is created.

Julie’s authority: Julie may compare forecasted reviewer actions with the intervention-authority map and release-gate design, reconstruct the chronology, distinguish prediction from observed action, identify missing or contradictory records, test legitimate explanations, and state bounded conclusions. She may not compel privileged or classified materials, declassify the assessment, direct interviews, identify a natural person from role metadata alone, determine legality, accuse sponsor-side participants, merge national repositories, or treat foresight as proof of hostile command.

Proof ceiling: The authenticated counterfactual review-response analysis proves that sponsor-side human actors prospectively considered defined reviewer objections and intervention measures and related them to the timing of integrated disclosure. It does not prove that the forecasts were accurate, that the reviewers would actually have acted as predicted, that the analysis was prepared to evade lawful review, that the gate was unlawful or deceptive, that every sponsor-side actor shared or understood the analysis, that a particular natural person possessed malicious intent, that the sponsor controlled every downstream action, that the escaped commander directed or controlled the process, or that common hostile command, organizational control, state sponsorship, criminal liability, or an ordered or attempted attack existed.

Technology rule: The assessment, decision-tree, versioning, routing, access, release-gate, and approval systems are non-sentient. Human actors create, authorize, revise, transmit, interpret, implement, misuse, conceal, preserve, and investigate the analysis and resulting process.

Narrative consequence: Julie can now establish prospective sponsor-side knowledge of the specific interventions meaningful integrated review was expected to produce. The central uncertainty shifts from what sponsor-side actors foresaw to whether those forecasts were used as a decision criterion when selecting the final release-gate architecture.

Open details: The assessment’s exact form, authorship and review roles, protected reviewer channels, forecasted objections, expected holds and safeguards, confidence levels, duration estimates, comments, rejected assumptions, privilege or classification disputes, distribution, redactions, first evidence that the forecast drove a design choice, and later evidence distinguishing lawful contingency planning from deliberate review avoidance or covert operational purpose remain unresolved. The 100,000-word hard minimum and 105,000–112,000-word planning target remain unchanged.

## Decision 121 — A Versioned Option-Selection Record Rejects Review-Preserving Alternatives and Selects the Gate That Defers the Forecasted Interventions

Selection: B.

Status: Locked.

Decision: The sponsor repository contains a versioned alternatives analysis, gate-design comparison, decision matrix, architecture-selection record, or equivalent human-authored planning object. It compares at least three materially different review architectures: early integrated review while relevant reviewers retain ordinary intervention authority; controlled but effective integrated review through protected access, redaction, cleared reviewers, a special integration panel, or an equivalent mechanism preserving meaningful intervention; and late informational release after local approvals, contracts, capacity reservations, access actions, or implementation milestones are substantially fixed and ordinary intervention powers have expired.

Comparative content: For each architecture, the record identifies the reviewer receiving the integrated information; the forecasted objection, hold, safeguard, escalation, consultation, disclosure, or reopening action; the authority supporting that response; the expected schedule, cost, confidentiality, contractual, capacity, and implementation consequences; the degree to which timely intervention is preserved or extinguished; and the relationship to the sponsor’s stated continuity objective.

Selection finding: The final version rejects the review-preserving alternatives and selects the late-release gate. The recorded rejection reasons depend, at least in part, on forecasted reviewer interventions or their consequences, including reopening approvals, loss of vendor capacity, restoration of resilience records or safeguards, qualification of protected assumptions, cross-national consultation, delay of the implementation window, exposure of protected methods or compartments, or transfer of decision authority to another lawful reviewer.

Authentication finding: Native version history, hashes, parent-package references, requirements-crosswalk identifiers, intervention-authority-map references, counterfactual-assessment identifiers, comments, resolution entries, approval records, access history, routing records, recipient copies, and the gate actually implemented establish that the option-selection record is authentic and corresponds to the architecture used. Similar wording, parallel timing, or an unsigned comparison is insufficient without reconciled lineage.

Causation boundary: The record proves that forecasted reviewer interventions were used as a criterion in comparative gate selection and that sponsor-side actors knowingly rejected alternatives preserving earlier integrated review and ordinary intervention authority. It does not automatically prove that avoidance of intervention was the sole, primary, or unlawful objective. Consideration, material reliance, decisive reliance, and exclusive purpose remain separate findings unless evidence expressly unites them.

Feasibility boundary: An alternative’s presence in the analysis does not establish that it was legally authorized, technically available, properly staffed, affordable, timely, or compatible with national compartment rules. Documented feasibility, authority, privilege, security, emergency-continuity, and jurisdictional defects remain controlling evidence limits.

Role-separation rule: Options analyst, forecast author, legal or operational adviser, gate designer, sponsor-side integration role, approving function, delegate, access custodian, local reviewer, contracting official, implementation actor, downstream beneficiary, and ultimate directing authority remain distinct evidentiary roles unless authenticated evidence connects them. Approval of the selected architecture does not identify the natural person who conceived it or prove that all participants shared one purpose.

Alternative explanations: Lawful transaction certainty, emergency continuity, protection of national compartments, avoidance of duplicative or inconsistent review, commercial finality, source protection, privilege, procurement constraints, classified-method protection, and concern that a proposed integration mechanism lacked lawful authority remain possible.

Jurisdiction and custody: Each lawful custodian authenticates and retains its own alternatives record, reviewer mandate, forecast, gate, approval, contracting, access, and implementation evidence. Canadian and United States authorities preserve separate repositories, witness processes, privileges, classification controls, disclosure rules, and custody chains. No universal evidence pool is created.

Julie’s authority: Julie may reconstruct the comparative decision, identify which alternatives preserved intervention, compare forecasted consequences with recorded rejection reasons, test the implemented gate against the selected option, distinguish consideration from reliance and purpose, preserve feasibility objections and alternative explanations, and state bounded conclusions. She may not compel protected decision records, determine legality, declassify material, direct interviews, identify a natural person from role metadata, accuse an actor or institution, or merge national repositories.

Proof ceiling: The authenticated option-selection record proves that sponsor-side human actors used forecasted reviewer interventions as a criterion when selecting the release gate and knowingly rejected alternatives that would have preserved earlier integrated review and ordinary intervention authority. It does not prove that the rejected alternatives were legally available or operationally feasible, that selecting the late gate was unlawful or deceptive, that the predicted reviewers would actually have intervened, that the selected architecture lacked a legitimate purpose, that every participant understood the combined degradation, that a particular natural person possessed malicious intent, that the sponsor controlled every downstream action, that the escaped commander directed or controlled the decision, or that common hostile command, organizational control, state sponsorship, criminal liability, or an ordered or attempted attack existed.

Technology rule: The comparison, forecasting, routing, access, signing, approval, contracting, and implementation systems are non-sentient. Human actors design, compare, authorize, reject, select, implement, interpret, misuse, conceal, preserve, and investigate the architectures and records.

Standstill rule: The narrow standstill established in Decision 106 remains unchanged. This decision does not expand its scope, duration, authority, or legal effect.

Narrative consequence: Julie can now prove comparative choice rather than mere awareness. Sponsor-side actors knew the forecasted review consequences, considered architectures preserving timely integrated intervention, rejected those alternatives for reasons materially tied to the predicted interventions, and selected the late-release gate. The central unresolved issue is whether preservation of the combined degradation outcome itself was an explicit selection requirement or merely a consequence of lawful finality and compartment-management choices.

Open details: The record’s form, option names, authorship and review roles, feasibility findings, reviewer mandates, forecast references, rejection language, weighting of intervention consequences, approval chain, redactions, privileges, classification disputes, recipient visibility, first evidence that preservation of the exact degradation criteria was a nonnegotiable selection requirement, and later evidence connecting that requirement to a natural person or higher directing authority remain unresolved. The 100,000-word hard minimum and 105,000–112,000-word planning target remain unchanged.

## Decision 122 — A Versioned Outcome-Preservation Constraint Set Tests Every Architecture Against the Exact Combined-Degradation Criteria

Selection: B.

Status: Locked.

Decision: The option-selection record established in Decision 121 carries an authenticated outcome-preservation constraint set, acceptance-test specification, architecture-validation matrix, or equivalent human-authored control object. It evaluates every proposed release-gate architecture against whether the exact combined-degradation criteria survive integrated review, changed terminology, vendor substitution, different commercial or contracting routes, separate national and institutional authorities, partial implementation changes, and reviewer safeguards or reopening demands.

Mandatory criteria: The constraint set requires preservation of the same critical time window, degradation thresholds, retired capacity flag, disputed boundary assumption, exclusion or deprioritization of resilience records, and survival of the cumulative effect across substituted vendors, authorities, labels, and contractual routes. A neutral label may change, but the measurable function must remain traceable to the sponsor-side objective matrix and requirements crosswalk.

Failure conditions: A proposed architecture fails if an effective reviewer can restore excluded resilience records; qualify, replace, or remove the disputed boundary assumption; remove the retired capacity flag; widen, shift, or otherwise defeat the critical window; reduce the required degradation threshold; require consolidated disclosure before finality; reopen or modify the downstream substitute provisions; prevent the separately approved criteria from recombining; or otherwise make the planned cumulative effect materially uncertain.

Architecture finding: Early integrated review and controlled-but-effective integrated review fail one or more mandatory preservation tests because they leave an authorized reviewer with meaningful power to alter, condition, suspend, reopen, or reject a protected criterion. The selected late-release gate is the only recorded architecture that satisfies every mandatory constraint while delaying the integrated view until ordinary intervention authority has expired or become practically ineffective.

Authentication finding: Native version history, hashes, parent-package references, objective-matrix identifiers, requirements-crosswalk mappings, intervention-authority-map references, counterfactual review-response identifiers, option-selection records, comments, approvals, access history, routing records, test outputs, and the implemented gate establish that the constraint set is authentic and governed the comparative evaluation. A pass label alone is insufficient without criterion-level reconciliation identifying the test, version, and basis for the result.

Traceability finding: Authenticated test results connect the sponsor-side objective matrix, translated requirements crosswalk, protected parent request and child tasking, extraction recipe, substitute-package provisions, predicted combined effect, and selected release gate. The evidence establishes a human-controlled chain from protected outcome criteria to the architecture chosen to preserve them.

Nonnegotiable-requirement finding: Preservation of the exact combined-degradation criteria was an explicit, mandatory architecture requirement rather than a secondary preference, incidental benefit, or untested assumption. The selected gate was acceptable because it uniquely preserved all required criteria against the forecasted interventions associated with meaningful integrated review.

Role-separation rule: Constraint author, acceptance-test designer, options analyst, forecast author, legal or operational adviser, gate designer, sponsor-side approver, access custodian, local reviewer, contracting official, implementation actor, downstream drafter, and ultimate beneficiary remain distinct evidentiary roles unless authenticated evidence connects them. Role-based approval or validation does not automatically identify the natural person who conceived, understood, or intended the requirement.

Alternative explanations: Legitimate scenario fidelity, continuity testing, resilience benchmarking, red-team methodology, adversarial planning, insurance modeling, classified-method protection, procurement consistency, transaction certainty, and testing of a lawful contingency architecture remain possible. A requirement that a modeled outcome remain stable across alternatives is not inherently unlawful or hostile.

Jurisdiction and custody: Each lawful custodian authenticates and retains its own constraint definitions, option records, reviewer mandates, test versions, approvals, access records, commercial instruments, and downstream artifacts. Canadian and United States authorities preserve separate repositories, legal powers, witnesses, privileges, classification controls, disclosure rules, and custody chains. No universal evidence pool is created.

Julie’s authority: Julie may reconstruct criterion-level traceability, compare proposed architectures against authenticated constraints, distinguish observed validation results from inferred purpose, identify missing test evidence, preserve legitimate explanations, and state bounded conclusions. She may not compel protected records, declassify sponsor material, direct interviews, identify a natural person from role metadata, declare the constraints unlawful, accuse the sponsor or downstream actors, or merge national repositories.

Proof ceiling: The authenticated outcome-preservation constraint set proves that preservation of the exact combined-degradation criteria was an explicit, nonnegotiable architecture requirement and that the selected late-release gate uniquely satisfied it. It does not prove that the criteria were malicious, that their stated purpose was false, that the rejected alternatives were legally or operationally feasible, that implementation was intended as an attack, that every participant understood the complete objective, that a particular natural person possessed malicious intent, that the sponsor controlled every downstream action, that the escaped commander directed or controlled the requirement, or that common hostile command, organizational control, state sponsorship, criminal liability, or an ordered or attempted attack existed.

Technology rule: The constraint, test, traceability, document, routing, access, signing, and approval systems are non-sentient. Human actors define, authorize, apply, interpret, revise, misuse, conceal, preserve, and investigate the criteria, tests, and resulting actions.

Narrative consequence: Julie can prove that the late-release architecture was required to preserve an exact functional outcome against changes meaningful reviewers were forecasted to impose. The central uncertainty shifts to whether sponsor-side actors actively monitored implementation and corrected deviations so that the required result would actually occur.

Open details: The constraint set’s form, criterion identifiers, mandatory and advisory classifications, test methods, architecture versions, pass-or-fail records, authorship and approval roles, redactions, compartment boundaries, disputed feasibility of rejected options, first evidence that the constraints governed live or staged implementation, and later evidence connecting outcome enforcement to knowledge, intent, or a higher directing authority remain unresolved. The 100,000-word hard minimum and 105,000–112,000-word planning target remain unchanged.

## Decision 123 — A Versioned Closed-Loop Acceptance-and-Correction Protocol Measures Every Criterion and Triggers Remedial Action

Selection: B.

Status: Locked.

Decision: The sponsor repository contains a versioned implementation-control protocol, acceptance-and-correction plan, commissioning specification, or equivalent human-authored record. It converts the outcome-preservation constraints established in Decision 122 from architecture tests into active implementation controls.

Protocol structure: For each protected criterion, the protocol identifies the implementation event or evidence source to be measured, the acceptable variance or tolerance, the required measurement point, the role responsible for reviewing the result, the record required to establish pass or failure, the corrective action available when the criterion is threatened, and the approval required before corrected implementation may proceed.

Operational linkage: The protocol links the constraints to authenticated implementation records, including contract and capacity milestones, vendor confirmations, dataset and extraction-recipe versions, temporary-access and certificate events, approval and release timestamps, cross-system timing, substitute-package provisions, and locally preserved performance or dependency findings. A summary pass label is insufficient without criterion-level traceability.

Closed-loop enforcement finding: When implementation diverges from a mandatory criterion, the protocol triggers a documented human response. Corrective action may include substituting a vendor or contractual route, reserving replacement capacity, reissuing an extract from the approved recipe version, correcting a field, exception, or source-data selection, resequencing an implementation step, delaying or repeating an approval action, revising a local provision while preserving its mapped function, or repeating validation until the criterion passes.

Causation requirement: Each material variance, review, direction, approval, corrective action, changed downstream artifact, and before-and-after result must be independently preserved. The finding does not rest merely on a final outcome matching the constraints. It rests on authenticated evidence that human actors detected departures and intervened to restore the required result.

Role-separation rule: Constraint author, monitoring designer, data custodian, variance reviewer, corrective-action authorizer, technical implementer, vendor, contracting official, local approver, sponsor-side integration role, certificate actor, downstream drafter, and ultimate beneficiary remain distinct unless authenticated evidence connects them. Protected-role activity is not natural-person attribution.

Alternative explanations: Lawful commissioning, quality assurance, emergency continuity, resilience testing, safety validation, contractual acceptance, regulatory compliance, protection of a classified scenario’s fidelity, ordinary requirements management, and correction of innocent implementation defects remain possible. Repeated preservation of the criteria does not alone establish hostile purpose.

Jurisdiction and custody: Each Canadian, United States, commercial, technical, regulatory, and contractual custodian authenticates and retains its own measurements, approvals, variance records, corrective directions, implementation changes, and resulting artifacts. National repositories, witness authorities, privileges, classification controls, disclosure rules, and custody chains remain separate. No universal evidence pool is created.

Julie’s authority: Julie may reconstruct criterion-level measurement and correction sequences, compare certified versions and hashes, distinguish final-state coincidence from causal intervention, identify missing links and inconsistent explanations, preserve role separation, and state bounded conclusions. She may not compel records, direct interviews, order corrective action, identify natural persons from role metadata alone, decide legality, merge national repositories, or treat operational enforcement as proof of hostile command.

Proof ceiling: The authenticated protocol and correction records prove that sponsor-side or authorized implementation actors actively measured the protected criteria and took human-directed remedial action when implementation threatened to depart from them. They do not prove that the preserved outcome was malicious, that every corrective actor understood the combined degradation, that any correction was unlawful, that one natural person controlled the entire loop, that implementation constituted an attack, that the sponsor controlled every downstream action, that the escaped commander directed or controlled the process, or that common hostile command, organizational control, state sponsorship, criminal liability, or an ordered or attempted attack existed.

Technology rule: Monitoring, workflow, access, routing, signing, validation, and approval systems are non-sentient. Human actors define the criteria, interpret measurements, authorize corrections, implement changes, preserve records, conceal conduct, and investigate the evidence.

Narrative consequence: Julie can now prove that the nonnegotiable criteria governed actual implementation through a closed human control loop. The investigation advances from architecture and forecast evidence to authenticated intervention in implementation records. The next unresolved issue is whether the corrective actions were parallel local quality-control responses or were coordinated through a common sponsor-side authority possessing the integrated view.

Open details: The protocol’s form, criterion identifiers, tolerances, measurement points, variance classifications, protected roles, correction thresholds, approval sequence, affected vendors and authorities, specific before-and-after records, failed or unresolved corrections, redactions, privilege and classification disputes, first evidence of common coordination across separate corrections, and later evidence connecting integrated operational control to natural-person knowledge or higher direction remain unresolved. The 100,000-word hard minimum and 105,000–112,000-word planning target remain unchanged.

## Decision 124 — A Versioned Integrated Variance-and-Remediation Ledger Coordinates Every Local Correction

Selection: B.

Status: Locked.

Decision: The sponsor repository contains a versioned integrated variance-and-remediation ledger, cross-system correction board, remediation map, or equivalent human-authored coordination object. It receives separately authenticated variance findings from multiple local vendors, authorities, contracting channels, technical systems, approval paths, and national repositories while retaining the complete relationship among those findings and the protected cumulative criteria established in Decisions 115 through 123.

Ledger function: For each local variance, the ledger identifies the affected protected criterion; records the local system, vendor, authority, contract, approval path, evidence source, and tolerance; evaluates the variance against the combined cross-system state; identifies dependencies on other corrections; sequences remedial actions so one local fix does not defeat another mandatory criterion; routes a bounded child instruction to the appropriate local actor; tracks acknowledgment, authority, implementation, and before-and-after evidence; and closes the variance only when the cumulative integrated state, rather than the local component alone, passes the protected acceptance tests.

Bounded-instruction rule: A local actor may receive only the instruction necessary to perform a specific correction, including replacement capacity, vendor or contract substitution, clause revision, extract reissue, field or exception correction, approval resequencing, or timing adjustment. The full integrated rationale and the relationship among separate corrections remain restricted to the sponsor-side integration role. Limited local visibility does not by itself prove deception, complicity, or knowledge of the combined degradation.

Authentication finding: The coordination relationship is authenticated through native ledger version history, hashes, criterion and protocol identifiers, parent-child references between ledger entries and local instructions, routing and transmission records, acknowledgments, approval events, recipient-side copies, implementation timestamps, before-and-after artifacts, and criterion-level correspondence between the ledger’s sequencing logic and the changes actually performed. A common label, timetable, or unsupported sponsor assertion is insufficient.

Common-coordination finding: The ledger establishes that a common sponsor-side authority possessed the integrated view and used it to receive, evaluate, sequence, authorize or route, monitor, and close separate local corrections. The corrections were not merely parallel quality-control actions. They formed one managed remediation sequence governed by the protected cumulative result.

Role-separation rule: Ledger author, integration-role holder, variance analyst, corrective-action authorizer, routing officer, access custodian, local recipient, technical implementer, vendor, contracting official, local approver, certificate actor, sponsor approver, and ultimate beneficiary remain distinct evidentiary roles unless authenticated evidence connects them. A role-bound ledger event or child instruction does not automatically identify a natural person.

Alternative explanations: Lawful program integration, complex commissioning, emergency continuity, cross-vendor quality assurance, safety coordination, classified scenario control, regulatory harmonization, management of interdependent infrastructure, and ordinary correction of dependencies remain possible. Integrated coordination and preserved outcome do not alone establish deception, illegality, or hostile purpose.

Jurisdiction and custody: The sponsor-side custodian authenticates and retains the integrated ledger and protected approvals. Each Canadian, United States, commercial, technical, regulatory, and contractual custodian separately authenticates its own variance report, child instruction, receipt, authority, correction, and resulting artifact. National repositories, witness authority, privileges, classification controls, disclosure rules, and custody chains remain separate. No universal evidence pool is created.

Julie’s authority: Julie may correlate certified criterion identifiers, hashes, parent-child references, correction dependencies, sequencing, acknowledgments, and before-and-after results; reconstruct what each participant could see; distinguish local action from integrated coordination; preserve competing explanations; identify missing links; and state bounded findings. She may not compel the sponsor ledger or personnel files, direct interviews, identify a natural person from role metadata, declassify protected rationale, accuse a participant, determine legality, merge national repositories, or treat common coordination as proof of hostile command.

Proof ceiling: The authenticated integrated variance-and-remediation ledger proves that a common sponsor-side authority possessed the integrated view and coordinated separate corrective actions to preserve the protected cumulative criteria. It does not prove that the coordination was unlawful, that the preserved result was malicious, that every local actor understood the combined effect, that one natural person controlled the ledger or all corrections, that implementation constituted an attack, that the sponsor controlled every downstream action, that the escaped commander directed or controlled the coordination process, or that common hostile command, organizational control, state sponsorship, criminal liability, or an ordered or attempted attack existed.

Technology rule: Ledger, routing, workflow, access, signing, monitoring, and approval systems are non-sentient. Human actors define, authorize, transmit, implement, interpret, misuse, conceal, preserve, and investigate the corrections and records.

Narrative consequence: Julie can now prove a common sponsor-side operational coordination process connecting separately innocent-looking local corrections to one integrated acceptance state. The central uncertainty shifts from whether the corrections were coordinated to who possessed authority over the integration role, what knowledge governed its live decisions, and whether the preserved cumulative result was a lawful protected objective or a concealed operational purpose.

Open details: The ledger’s form, repository, protected criterion identifiers, integration role, access boundaries, variance classes, child-instruction format, approval thresholds, correction dependencies, national and commercial custodians, missing entries, redactions, privileges, classification barriers, first evidence connecting live ledger decisions to a higher authorizing role, and later evidence distinguishing lawful integrated control from deceptive or hostile direction remain unresolved. The 100,000-word hard minimum and 105,000–112,000-word planning target remain unchanged.

## Decision 125 — A Versioned Exception-and-Escalation Matrix Produces Hash-Bound Higher-Role Dispositions for Live Material Variances

Selection: B.

Status: Locked.

Decision: The integrated variance-and-remediation ledger established in Decision 124 is governed by a versioned exception-and-escalation matrix, material-variance protocol, decision-threshold map, or equivalent human-authored control object. The matrix limits the sponsor-side integration role’s ordinary discretion whenever a live variance threatens a protected criterion or combination of criteria beyond a defined tolerance.

Decision-rights structure: For every material variance class, the matrix identifies the affected protected criterion or criteria, the tolerance within which the integration role may act, the threshold requiring mandatory escalation, the higher protected authorizing function, the analyses and alternatives required in the escalation packet, whether implementation or closure must pause, the dispositions available to the higher function, and the evidence required before the ledger may resume or close the variance.

Live-use finding: At least one independently authenticated variance crosses a mandatory threshold. The ledger records the breach, freezes ordinary closure or corrective discretion, and generates a versioned escalation package bound by hashes or immutable identifiers to the relevant ledger entry, protected criteria, implementation records, variance evidence, proposed remedies, and considered alternatives. The package is routed to the specified higher protected authorizing function.

Higher-role disposition: The higher function returns a role-bound, signed, or equivalently authenticated disposition. The disposition may approve or reject the proposed correction; require a different vendor, contract route, capacity source, extract, clause, sequence, or timing; preserve a particular assumption or exclusion; direct additional testing; suspend implementation; or require renewed evidence. The integration role may implement only the authorized disposition and may close the variance only after the combined state satisfies the disposition’s stated conditions.

Authentication requirement: A governance label, unsigned note, automated status, or later summary is insufficient. The finding requires the live threshold calculation, matrix version, frozen-status record, escalation-package hashes, routing history, role-bound disposition, resulting child instruction, local implementation evidence, retest results, and final ledger closure to reconcile in sequence.

Control finding: The evidence establishes actual higher-role control of specified live material remediation decisions, not merely the existence of a general charter or supervisory relationship. The integration role remains the coordinator of bounded local instructions, but its discretion is suspended at the authenticated threshold and restored only within the returned disposition.

Role-separation rule: Matrix author, threshold analyst, ledger operator, escalation-package author, higher authorizing function, role delegate, disposition signer, routing officer, local recipient, technical implementer, vendor, contracting official, certificate actor, sponsor approver, and ultimate beneficiary remain distinct evidentiary roles unless authenticated evidence connects them. A role-bound disposition does not by itself identify the natural person who exercised the role.

Alternative explanations: Lawful safety governance, classified-program control, continuity oversight, national-security review, contractual-risk management, regulatory compliance, procurement supervision, and ordinary governance of interdependent infrastructure remain possible. Mandatory escalation and higher-role intervention are not inherently improper.

Jurisdiction and custody: The sponsor-side custodian authenticates the matrix, threshold record, escalation package, routing history, disposition, and ledger response. Each local, commercial, technical, regulatory, Canadian, and United States custodian separately authenticates its received child instruction, authority, implementation change, and resulting evidence. Separate repositories, custody chains, privileges, classification controls, witness processes, and disclosure rules remain intact.

Julie’s authority: Julie may reconstruct the threshold-to-escalation-to-disposition-to-implementation chain, compare certified hashes and role records, distinguish formal authority from exercised authority, identify missing or inconsistent links, preserve competing explanations, and state bounded conclusions. She may not compel protected records, identify a natural person from role metadata alone, direct interviews, declassify material, determine legality, accuse the higher function, or merge Canadian and United States evidence holdings.

Proof ceiling: The authenticated exception-and-escalation matrix and returned dispositions prove that a higher protected authorizing function exercised control over specified live material remediation decisions and that the integration role implemented those decisions through the ledger.
