Accountability for earlier conduct. The appointment does not erase, ratify, condemn, or prejudge any legitimate question concerning Elias’s earlier methods, access, credentials, disclosures, handling of material, or technical statements. Those matters remain subject to the appropriate administrative, professional, contractual, security, or legal review. Elias must fully disclose methods and access relevant to the assigned work. A failure to disclose, material departure from scope, use of unauthorized credentials, destruction or alteration risk, undisclosed conflict, or unsupported representation may suspend or terminate the appointment and trigger referral.

Conversely, institutional disagreement with a documented technical conclusion is not itself misconduct. Elias retains the right to record a good-faith dissent, and the institution retains the right to challenge his work through replication, peer review, and a separately constituted examination. His appointment may be suspended when a live conflict or safety issue requires it, but suspension is not automatically a finding of wrongdoing.

Technical-report structure. Every material Elias report must separately identify the question authorized; the material actually examined; the custody and derivation of that material; the method, tool versions, and test environment; directly observed results; reproducibility status; confidence and limitations; alternative explanations; unresolved contradictions; actions that would require separate authority; and propositions the result cannot establish. His report enters the protected record independently of Julie’s assessment. Neither report silently incorporates the other as an independent source.

Suspense effects and legitimate alternatives. The appointment may be genuine reform, a way of obtaining difficult expertise without surrendering institutional control, an effort to restore credibility, a controlled test of Elias’s reliability, a means of monitoring him, or several of these at once. Technical anomalies remain capable of supporting multiple explanations. A mismatch may reflect ordinary system variance, incomplete acquisition, tool-version differences, time-synchronization error, contamination, benign maintenance or testing, an unrelated third-party modification, deliberate separation or alteration, or a genuinely different component or system.

Resistance to Elias’s work may reflect legitimate source protection, security restrictions, evidentiary risk, professional disagreement, institutional embarrassment, or self-interested obstruction. The story may generate suspense from the resistance without treating it as automatic proof of conspiracy. Agreement among reviewers may reflect genuine replication, shared assumptions, common source dependence, or institutional convergence; the architecture requires the basis to be documented rather than presumed.

Personal continuation. Elias accepts the appointment only on the condition that the work remains bounded, reviewable, and separate from his relationship with Julie. He and Julie choose to continue their personal relationship without using it as a private evidentiary channel. Either may refuse a professional assignment that would make the relationship incompatible with independent review. Their continuing bond therefore carries emotional consequence into the series while preserving the institutional boundaries established by the corrected investigation.

Proof ceiling: Elias’s independent technical-audit appointment, supervised access, documented methods, preserved dissent, and resulting reports establish only that Canada has authorized him to examine defined technical-integrity questions under controlled conditions and that his findings form part of the protected review record. They do not establish that every Elias conclusion is correct, give him independent access to any original, device, account, credential, live system, repository, or foreign record, authorize him to operate, activate, probe, modify, exploit, or connect recovered materials, make his findings the sole basis for search, seizure, detention, charging, attribution, or operational action, resolve whether any earlier Elias conduct was proper or improper, establish bad faith by another examiner or institution, identify who controlled the unrecovered operational core, or prove harmful intent, component or package operability, activation, use, an attempted attack, parent-actor involvement, Western Pacific control, common command, or state sponsorship.

Forward target: Decision 146 — the final commander and custodian statuses, institutional consequences, protected findings, public-summary limits, and repository and disposition consequences after the corrected investigation.

## Decision 146 — Differentiated Final-Status Matrix, Protected Institutional Findings, Narrow Public Accountability Record, and Segmented Repository Disposition

Selection: B.

Status: Locked.

Core architecture. Canada closes the corrected investigation through six deliberately separated products and decisions: individualized final-status rulings; a protected institutional findings record; a bounded public accountability summary; segmented repository and evidence disposition; documented Canadian, United States, and Western Pacific corrections; and explicit evidentiary conditions for any later investigation. The result does not declare every person innocent, identify a complete conspiracy, or treat the operational core’s continued absence as proof against the commander. It states what the Canadian record supports now, what it no longer supports, and what lawful consequences follow.

Final commander status. Any package-based detention, investigative restraint, or adverse classification ends unless a separate and independently sufficient Canadian legal basis exists. The commander is released from component-based custody and may be approached only through a documented voluntary-witness, represented-subject, or protected-witness process appropriate to the question actually being asked. Any continuing obligation must possess its own factual and legal foundation. The final Canadian record expressly does not treat the commander as the proven controller, transferor, intended user, or post-separation director of the unrecovered operational core.

The protected finding preserves that authentic package evidence is consistent with commander-level interaction or custody during the bounded handoff. It also records that the authenticated post-separation path does not establish that the commander retained, transferred, concealed, destroyed, or directed the missing component after separation. Release is not a declaration of complete innocence, and earlier package interaction is not converted into proof of guilt.

Final custodian and intermediary statuses. Every immediate custodian, intermediary, records holder, and route-linked person receives a separate written disposition tied to that person’s evidence. An over-targeted custodian whose priority depended on recycled association, unsupported possession theories, or evidence contradicted by the authenticated transfer path is released or reclassified. The negative result and current status must propagate through Canadian and approved joint repositories so that the superseded theory cannot remain active in another product, alert, or jurisdiction without new evidence.

A custodian or intermediary against whom specific authenticated evidence remains may be subject only to the process supported by that evidence. Possible steps include a voluntary or lawfully compelled interview, a narrowly bounded preservation request, an application for a specific search, a professional or administrative referral, a charging assessment concerning a separately identifiable act, or protective measures when cooperation creates a credible risk. No status is inherited from the commander, another custodian, the political importance of the case, or the continued absence of the core.

An act of isolating, transferring, retaining, routing, or preserving material remains analytically separate from its purpose. The person may have acted for harmful, self-protective, protective, bureaucratic, technical-safety, evidentiary, coercive, commercial, or unrelated reasons. None of those alternatives is resolved merely by proving the act.

Protected institutional findings. Canada creates a final protected findings record identifying what the recovered package authentically establishes; what the partial segment, interface artifact, receipt, container evidence, routing remnant, protected-intermediary record, or derivative record establishes; the authenticated portion of the post-separation path; which theories were disproven, narrowed, superseded, or left unresolved; which custody and investigative decisions changed and why; where circular corroboration, provenance failure, overstatement, repository contamination, or cross-border assumption occurred; which investigative and technical methods remain valid; which institutional or professional questions warrant a separate process; and which propositions cannot presently be resolved.

Julie’s evidentiary-integrity report and Elias’s technical-audit report remain separate source records. The protected findings may cross-reference them but may not merge them into one supposedly independent source, obscure their provenance, or conceal a material disagreement. Dissents, limitations, corrections, negative findings, and unresolved contradictions remain attached to the record and visible to authorized future reviewers.

Institutional consequences. Canada imposes proportionate correction and review rather than a predetermined purge. Consequences may include correction of investigative and intelligence records; review of supervisors who approved unsupported status extensions; repository, provenance, and version-control reforms; technical-method validation requirements; retraining concerning circular corroboration and foreign-source attribution; professional or security referrals based on identified conduct; review of disclosure failures; preservation for litigation or oversight; and formal acknowledgment that negative evidence changed the case. A process defect does not automatically establish bad faith. A referral does not establish misconduct. Institutional embarrassment does not justify suppressing a validated correction.

Bounded public accountability summary. After legal, privacy, security, fair-process, and foreign-disclosure review, Canada publishes a narrow factual summary. It states that an authentic dual-purpose package and related records were recovered under Canadian authority; the immediate Canadian operation ended without proof of the broader claims initially feared; a material operational component remains unrecovered; a bounded post-separation path and partial artifact or derivative record were authenticated; individualized custody decisions were corrected where the evidence required; no public finding establishes that an uncharged person controlled the missing core; institutional review and evidence-integrity measures have begun; and foreign-origin allegations remain attributed to their actual sources and jurisdictions.

The public summary does not identify an uncharged intermediary or protected witness; expose operational methods, credentials, repository locations, or foreign-restricted material; disclose the complete package architecture; state that the commander was completely exonerated; imply that institutional review proves misconduct; or attribute the package or missing core to a state or higher actor. Where a previously public or widely circulated allegation materially affected a person and is no longer supported, the correction must be meaningful enough to prevent the obsolete allegation from remaining the only accessible account.

Repository and disposition architecture. Canada retains the original recovered package and every Canadian original that remains evidentially material under separate, documented Canadian custody. Original physical evidence, forensic acquisitions, verified working copies, Elias’s technical-audit materials, Julie’s protected review materials, status and legal-process records, foreign-supplied records, public-summary source material, and disproven or superseded assessments remain distinguishable by repository, provenance, authority, and access history.

Repositories preserve provenance and correction history. A disproven theory is not silently deleted in a way that obscures how earlier decisions were made. It is visibly marked as superseded, its downstream use is corrected, and access is limited according to continuing legal need. Over-collected, irrelevant, privileged, or minimized data is returned, destroyed, sealed, or access-restricted when its lawful retention basis ends. Litigation, oversight, disclosure, safety, and review holds remain separately documented. Preservation of a record does not preserve an adverse status against the person from whom it was obtained.

The partial segment, interface artifact, recovered package, and any later candidate remain physically and digitally separate. They may not be connected, merged, activated, repaired, or operationally tested merely to determine whether they function together. Any later comparison requires a new defined purpose, safety assessment, authority, supervised method, and independent custody record.

United States consequences. The United States receives an approved derivative package containing authenticated findings Canada may lawfully share, corrected status and provenance information relevant to American records, technical indicators that may be examined under American authority, and express limits on what the Canadian evidence establishes. The United States receives no ownership or constructive custody of Canadian originals, no general repository access, and no power to direct Canadian retention, examination, release, or public disclosure. American authorities independently decide whether their own records, persons, repositories, or processes require correction or action.

Western Pacific consequences. Each Western Pacific partner receives only the information approved for that partner and a bounded correction when Canadian information materially affected a partner assessment. A partner independently determines the treatment of its own records, persons, and repositories. A refusal to correct, disclose, or cooperate is documented but does not authorize Canada to alter the foreign record or infer concealment or bad faith. No joint record may imply that multiple jurisdictions independently corroborated a proposition when each received it from the same originating source.

Julie’s role. Julie completes the protected evidentiary-integrity findings and verifies the proposition map underlying the public summary. She ensures that authenticated fact is separated from inference; source lineage is visible; corrected statuses appear in every relevant Canadian product; negative evidence has operational effect; unresolved attribution remains unresolved; and institutional recommendations do not become findings of guilt. Julie may identify an issue, preserve the evidence, and recommend the proper forum. She does not determine criminal, disciplinary, employment, compensation, classification, political, or foreign consequences.

Elias’s role. Elias completes a separate technical-audit record specifying what was examined; what is reproducible; which technical characterizations are reliable; where generic compatibility was overstated; which comparison methods could alter, activate, or destroy evidence; what remains technically unresolved; and which future evidentiary event could justify renewed examination. He may review the technical accuracy of public language within his assignment, but he does not control the repositories, determine general public wording, authorize another jurisdiction’s use, or acquire continuing access by virtue of his appointment. A documented disagreement with another examiner or institution is preserved and is not itself misconduct.

Narrative consequences. Book 2 resolves through evidence changing real human and institutional outcomes rather than through a second tactical climax. The commander and over-targeted custodians leave procedural limbo. A narrowly supported intermediary thread may remain, but only at the level the evidence supports. Julie and Elias see that their work changed custody decisions, corrected the shared record, and constrained what institutions may lawfully claim. Their victory is a truthful boundary, not a complete geopolitical solution.

Structural consequences and later-investigation threshold. The immediate Canadian operation is over; final individual statuses are known; institutional errors and unresolved questions are separately recorded; Julie and Elias retain distinct, bounded roles; the package and derivative evidence have stable repository homes; and the public and protected accounts are separated. Book 2 may not be reopened on unchanged speculation. A later component investigation requires a genuinely new evidentiary trigger, such as authenticated recovery of the operational core, a new component-specific identifier, a verified repository event, a corroborated transfer record, a foreign disclosure lawfully supplied, or evidence tying a specific actor to control, use, or direction.

Suspense and legitimate alternatives. An institutional failure may have arisen from urgency, fragmented information, confirmation bias, ordinary error, source protection, professional disagreement, bureaucratic self-interest, deliberate manipulation, or several causes operating together. The authenticated post-separation path may reflect deliberate removal, safety isolation, an improvised transfer, a protective act, evidentiary preservation, an unrelated technical process, coercion, or a route whose final destination remains unknown. Foreign nondisclosure may reflect sovereignty, source protection, an ongoing investigation, different legal rules, institutional embarrassment, or concealment. The unrecovered core remains consequential as a possibility, not as a presumed active weapon or confirmed attack system.

Proof ceiling: Final individualized status decisions, protected institutional findings, bounded public corrections, and segmented repository dispositions establish only the lawful status of each person on the evidence presently available, the authenticated facts and documented process defects specifically recorded, and the authorities under which Canada preserves, shares, corrects, returns, or disposes of the resulting material. They do not establish complete innocence or guilt of any commander, custodian, intermediary, examiner, or official; convert a process defect, status correction, referral, dissent, or nondisclosure into proof of bad faith or criminal misconduct; make Julie’s or Elias’s findings conclusive; authorize Canada, the United States, or any Western Pacific partner to exceed its own jurisdiction; identify who controlled or received the unrecovered operational core; or prove harmful intent, component or package operability, activation, use, an attempted attack, parent-actor involvement, Western Pacific control, common command, or state sponsorship.

Forward target: Decision 147 — the final scene, the precisely bounded series-level unresolved thread, and the evidentiary launch condition that may begin Book 3 without reopening Book 2 on unchanged speculation.

## Decision 147 — A Partner-Authenticated Foreign Repository Event Creates a New Case After Canadian Closure

Selection: B.

Status: Locked.

The Book 2 closing sequence first completes the Canadian resolution. The individualized commander and custodian status decisions have taken effect. The bounded public correction has been approved or released. Julie’s protected evidentiary-integrity findings and Elias’s separate technical-audit findings have been entered into their distinct controlled repositories, with preserved provenance, access history, limitations, dissent, and future-review conditions.

Julie and Elias leave the controlled records area without carrying original evidence, private credentials, undeclared technical material, or authority beyond their completed assignments. Their personal connection remains distinct from their institutional roles. Book 2 therefore reaches genuine professional, institutional, and emotional closure before the series-level hook appears.

Only after the Book 2 file is formally placed in its corrected posture does a designated Canadian authority receive a bounded notice from a Western Pacific partner. The partner reports a newly occurring repository, accession, quarantine, custody, or evidence-preservation event within its own jurisdiction. The approved derivative contains two independently documented characteristics sufficient to distinguish the event from generic technical similarity. At least one characteristic must be a component-specific identifier or an authenticated relationship to the recovered-package record; the other must be a distinctive interface, custody, routing, or preservation characteristic independently documented through the existing Canadian evidence architecture.

The notice does not show the operational core functioning, identify a human controller, establish that the physical core is present, or prove that any person or institution knowingly possesses it. It establishes only that a new authenticated event occurred in a foreign repository or custody system and that the event is sufficiently connected to the existing record to justify a separately authorized inquiry.

The Western Pacific partner retains the original record, object, system, personnel, searches, and legal authority. Canada receives only the derivative the partner is permitted to provide. The United States receives nothing automatically. No jurisdiction may treat Canadian receipt as a substitute for its own authority.

Canada opens a new assessment or assistance file bearing a new date, evidentiary origin, source lineage, purpose, and authority line. The event is not appended as an extension of the former commander case and cannot retroactively corroborate any superseded commander or custodian theory. The governing principle is: new evidence, new authority, new case.

Julie receives no automatic access merely because the event concerns the same technical architecture. A Canadian authority may give her a new written evidentiary-integrity assignment limited to provenance, source independence, circularity, corrections, alternative explanations, and the boundary between the new event and the closed Book 2 record. She may not direct the foreign partner, compel disclosure, determine a foreign custodian’s identity, or convert technical association into human attribution.

Elias receives no automatic technical role. A separate written assignment may authorize him to determine whether the supplied characteristics are distinctive, reproducible, and genuinely related to the authenticated Canadian record. He may not probe the foreign repository, use private credentials, contact a foreign system, examine an original outside the partner’s authority, or treat a derivative as equivalent to direct examination. His earlier report cannot become independent corroboration merely because the partner used characteristics derived from it.

The new event may concern the original operational core, a copy, a related component, an archival replay, a planted decoy, a compromised repository, a safety-isolation action, evidentiary preservation by an unknown custodian, or ordinary reuse of a technical interface. Delay or limits in partner disclosure may reflect authentication work, domestic law, source protection, sovereignty, technical caution, an ongoing investigation, bureaucratic delay, institutional embarrassment, or concealment. None of those alternatives is resolved by the notice itself.

Book 3 may begin only after Canada authenticates the derivative sufficiently to support a lawful next step and separately authorizes the people, methods, information, and jurisdiction involved. Possible opening questions include whether the event can be independently authenticated; whether Canada may receive additional derivatives; whether it concerns the original core, a copy, a decoy, or a related system; whether any person or institution may lawfully be linked to it; whether Julie or Elias receives a new written assignment; and whether the United States has an independent basis for involvement.

Proof ceiling: A partner-authenticated repository, accession, quarantine, custody, or preservation event and the authorized derivative connecting that event to distinctive characteristics in the existing Canadian record establish only that a new event occurred within the partner’s jurisdiction and that the event may support a separately authorized inquiry. They do not establish that the unrecovered operational core itself is present, intact, operable, or controlled by the repository, account, institution, or person associated with the event; identify a human controller, transferor, author, or intended user; make the event harmful rather than archival, protective, deceptive, compromised, or unrelated; authorize Canadian or United States access or action; revive any superseded commander or custodian theory; or prove harmful intent, activation, use, an attempted attack, parent-actor involvement, Western Pacific control, common command, or state sponsorship.

Forward target: Decision 148 — the comprehensive contradiction, continuity, proof-ceiling, suspense, character, jurisdiction, technical, custody, chronology, and completeness audit that determines whether Book 2 architecture may be formally closed or whether one bounded repair decision is genuinely necessary.

## Decision 148 — Comprehensive Material-Defect Audit, Formal Architecture Closure, and a Non-Canonical Drafting Watchlist

Selection: A.

Status: Locked.

The comprehensive material-defect audit covers the complete locked Book 2 architecture through Decision 147. The review tests the premise and central conflict; act-level escalation; chronology, travel, and physical feasibility; character knowledge; clue provenance and source independence; technical capability and limitations; Canadian, United States, and Western Pacific jurisdiction; preservation, search, seizure, custody, examination, exploitation, and sharing authority; package and missing-component continuity; Julie’s and Elias’s arcs; commander, custodian, and intermediary statuses; protected and public institutional outcomes; suspense and legitimate alternatives; proof-ceiling consistency; Book 2 resolution; and the bounded Book 3 launch.

A material defect exists only when the locked architecture contains an internal contradiction, impossible chronology or physical sequence, impossible character knowledge, action unsupported by governing authority, technical result exceeding established capability, custody or provenance break undermining a required finding, proof-ceiling conflict, unresolved primary Book 2 obligation, ending dependent on evidence never established, or a Book 3 hook that reopens Book 2 without genuinely new evidence.

The audit distinguishes material architecture defects from drafting-stage implementation risks and deliberately unresolved series questions. Naming refinements, scene order within compatible bounds, emotional emphasis, chapter balance, exposition management, procedural compression, and prose execution remain downstream choices unless they would alter locked canon. Deliberately unresolved questions concerning the operational core, later custodians, higher actors, common command, and state sponsorship are not defects.

Audit result: no material architecture defect was identified.

The closing chain is coherent. Decision 143 supplies a partial but consequential Canadian recovery, a Tier 1 post-separation route, a status correction for an over-targeted custodian, and an unrecovered operational core. Decisions 144 and 145 give Julie and Elias separate, time-limited, bounded Canadian roles without granting either tactical, prosecutorial, forensic-control, intelligence, credential, repository, or foreign-command authority. Decision 146 provides individualized final statuses, protected institutional findings, a bounded public accountability record, and segmented repository disposition. Decision 147 closes the Canadian case before a partner-authenticated Western Pacific repository event creates a separate possible Book 3 case under new evidence and fresh authority.

The jurisdictional architecture is coherent. Canada retains Canadian legal, tactical, custodial, forensic, interview, repository, and territorial authority. United States participation remains request-based, derivative, and non-command. Each Western Pacific partner retains control of its originals, repositories, personnel, examinations, disclosures, and legal process. No jurisdiction’s warrant, investigative label, custody decision, or technical conclusion substitutes for another jurisdiction’s authority.

The evidence and custody architecture is coherent. The recovered package, partial segment or interface artifact, transfer records, forensic acquisitions, working copies, Julie’s review materials, Elias’s audit materials, foreign-derived records, and superseded assessments remain separately identified and controlled. The package and any component candidate may not be connected, activated, merged, repaired, or operationally tested without new purpose, authority, safety review, custody documentation, and supervised method. Recovery does not equal authentication; technical relationship does not equal human attribution; preservation does not equal access; sharing does not transfer ownership.

The character architecture is coherent. Julie’s agency operates through provenance analysis, contradiction preservation, source-independence review, negative-evidence propagation, protected findings, and bounded recommendations. She does not become a tactical commander, arresting officer, prosecutor, package operator, foreign director, or permanent series-wide authority. Elias remains consequential through transparent technical characterization, reproducibility review, safe-method design, and protected dissent. He does not become an unofficial system key, private credential broker, sole critical witness, unsupervised examiner, operator, or unrestricted repository authority. Any Book 3 involvement by either character requires a new written assignment.

The commander and custodian architecture is coherent. Authentic package interaction remains separate from possession, concealment, transfer, intent, and control of the missing operational core. Negative and contradictory evidence changes investigative priority and individual status. Release or reclassification does not establish complete innocence, and continuing evidence preservation does not establish continuing suspicion. No person inherits status from another person or from the importance of the missing component.

The suspense architecture is coherent. Book 2 materially resolves the Canadian operation, the bounded package finding, the partial recovery, the corrected human consequences, the institutional record, and the principal character arcs. The operational core and higher architecture remain precisely bounded series questions. The Decision 147 foreign repository event is new evidence in a separate jurisdiction; it does not retroactively corroborate the closed commander theory or automatically give Canada, the United States, Julie, or Elias new access or authority.

A non-canonical drafting watchlist is established for downstream work:

• avoid repetitive procedural explanation while preserving meaningful authority distinctions;

• keep Julie active and decisive without granting command or legal powers;

• keep Elias technically consequential without private credentials, magical access, or sole-source conclusions;

• sustain tension through custody reviews, protected findings, technical disagreement, and institutional correction;

• clearly distinguish protected findings from the bounded public summary;

• preserve source lineage and prevent recycled claims from appearing independently corroborated;

• prevent the Western Pacific repository event from becoming premature attribution, proof of an active weapon, or a second Book 2 climax;

• deliver emotional and institutional closure before the Book 3 hook;

• maintain benign, protective, mistaken, technical, coercive, and deceptive alternatives until evidence distinguishes them.

The drafting watchlist is advisory and non-canonical. It may guide act, chapter, scene, drafting, continuity, and revision work but may not silently modify a locked decision.

Decision 149 is authorized only if later verification identifies one concrete material contradiction that cannot be resolved through faithful implementation of existing canon. The present audit identifies no such defect. No Decision 149 is required or authorized by the current record.

Book 2 story architecture is formally closed. The governing architecture now authorizes movement into detailed act architecture, chapter architecture, scene architecture, drafting, continuity validation, developmental revision, and publication production. Any later change to locked canon requires an explicit reopening decision grounded in a newly identified material contradiction or a deliberate user-directed canon revision.

Proof ceiling: Completion of the comprehensive material-defect audit and formal architecture closure establishes only that the locked Book 2 architecture is internally coherent, sufficiently complete for downstream act, chapter, scene, drafting, and production work, and free of any identified material contradiction requiring another canon decision. It does not establish that every drafting choice has been predetermined, eliminate the need for later continuity or prose review, convert a drafting watchlist into canon, establish the truth of any unresolved allegation, identify the controller or location of the unrecovered operational core, or prove harmful intent, component or package operability, activation, use, an attempted attack, parent-actor involvement, Western Pacific control, common command, or state sponsorship.

Closure target: Book 2 story architecture is formally closed. No Decision 149 is authorized because the audit identified no material architecture defect. The governing documents now authorize movement into detailed act, chapter, scene, drafting, continuity-validation, developmental-revision, and publication-production work. Any later change to locked canon requires an explicit reopening decision grounded in a newly identified material contradiction or a deliberate user-directed canon revision.

Decision 149 — User-Authorized Reconciliation of Act V Word Budget and Scientist POV Allocation

Selection: A — preserve the 38-unit/five-act architecture, raise only the Act V implementation range, and restore the scientist’s locked POV requirement through secondary/co-primary scenes inside existing chapters.

Status: locked.

Authority or source: Explicit user direction on July 25, 2026 to override the documented downstream conflicts and continue.

Exact decision:

1. Act V, Chapters 30–37, is revised from 21,000–22,000 words to an implementation range of 23,700–24,500 words. The exact scene-architecture working allocation is 24,000 words. The five-act chapter allocation remains Prologue–6, 7–13, 14–21, 22–29, and 30–37.

2. The exact whole-book working allocation is 109,800 words: Act I 18,500; Act II 20,000; Act III 23,000; Act IV 24,300; Act V 24,000. The hard minimum of 100,000 and planning target of 105,000–112,000 remain unchanged.

3. The scientist receives controlled POV scenes in seven existing chapters totaling exactly 10,000 planned words: Chapter 2, 900; Chapter 6, 1,200; Chapter 10, 1,500; Chapter 14, 1,300; Chapter 18, 1,700; Chapter 20, 1,300; Chapter 21, 2,100. These are secondary or co-primary POV scenes within the existing 38 narrative units and do not add chapters.

4. Scientist POV may show captivity, coercion, resistance, provenance signaling, bounded technical knowledge, and recovery. It may not reveal commander identity, exact holding location to Julie before lawful verification, final package route, protected facts unavailable to investigators, or any conclusion above the existing proof ceilings.

5. The Chapter Architecture statement allocating only 5,000–7,000 scientist words is superseded. The primary-POV labels for Chapters 2, 6, 10, 14, 18, and 20 are clarified to include secondary scientist POV; Chapter 21 remains scientist/Elias.

6. Scene-level target distribution is planning architecture, not manuscript prose and not a requirement that final drafted chapters hit the target word exactly. Drafting remains subject to the locked chapter ranges, proof ceilings, and separate approval workflow.

Reason: The chapter ranges for Act V had a minimum total of 23,700 words, exceeding the former 22,000-word act ceiling, and the scientist POV allocation contradicted the earlier locked five-to-seven-chapter/10,000–14,000-word requirement. This bounded repair makes faithful scene architecture mathematically possible while preserving the story, chapter count, revelation order, jurisdiction, evidence, custody, technical, character, ending, and Book 3 boundaries.

Canon consequences: Decisions 001–148 remain controlling except for the two expressly superseded downstream implementation provisions above. No premise, revelation, proof ceiling, authority limit, evidence order, custody rule, status outcome, repository disposition, ending condition, or Book 3 launch condition changes.

Files affected: 03 - Book 2 Decision Log & Canon Ledger; 04 - Book 2 Premise & Story Architecture Lock; 05 - Book 2 Act Architecture; 06 - Book 2 Chapter Architecture; 07 - Book 2 Scene Architecture and Chapter Mission Locks.

Supersedes: Only the former Act V range of 21,000–22,000 words and the Chapter Architecture’s 5,000–7,000-word scientist allocation/omitted secondary POV labels.

Open questions: None required to complete scene architecture. Proper names, researched engineering specifics, and final prose execution remain downstream.
